where
\mathcal{F}(-\times\Delta^{m})\simeq f_{m*}f_{m}^{*}\mathcal{F}
using the projection
\Delta^{m}_{\mathrm{geom}}\rightarrow S
.
The geometric realization of
\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathcal{D}}_{\bullet}
(namely the colimit over
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}
) is denoted by
\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathcal{D}}
.
When
\mathcal{D}=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we simply denote
\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(\bullet)}
by
\mathrm{Sus}_{(\bullet)}
.

Remark
. —

1.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an
abelian
presheaf, namely
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\heartsuit}
.
Since for any morphism
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
, the pushforward
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}(Y)

is t-exact,
\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}_{m}(\mathcal{F})
remains to be an abelian presheaf,
and equivalent to
\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathrm{Ab}}_{m}(\mathcal{F})
considered as an object of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S})
.
As a convention, we denote
\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}(\mathcal{F})
(not
\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathrm{Ab}}(\mathcal{F})
) by
\mathrm{Sus}(\mathcal{F})
.

2.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant
\mathcal{D}
-valued presheaf. Then the canonical morphism
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{F})

is an equivalence.
Indeed, for any edge
e
of
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}
, the map
\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathcal{D}}_{\bullet}(e)(\mathcal{F})
is an equivalence.
Thus, by
[
HTT
, 4.4.4.10]
(as well as
[
HTT
, 5.5.8.4, 5.5.8.7]
to see that
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}
is weakly contractible),

\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{F})
.

3.

Even though we do not use this, we may check that
\mathrm{Sus}^{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{F})
is an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant
\mathcal{D}
-valued presheaf.
Moreover, a
\mathcal{D}
-valued presheaf is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant if and only if the canonical morphism
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sus}(\mathcal{F})
is an equivalence.

1.16 Lemma
. —

Let
i\colon\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
be a morphism in
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S})
,
and assume we are given a retract
r\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
.
Furthermore, assume there exists the following homotopy commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{adj}_{p}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{adj}_{p}}
\textstyle{p_{*}p^{*}\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{p_{*}p^{*}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\textstyle{p_{*}p^{*}\mathcal{G}}

such that the composition
\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{c}p_{*}p^{*}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{i_{x}}\mathcal{F}
,
using the notation of Definition
1.5
,
is homotopic to
i\circ r
when
x=0
and homotopic to the identity when
x=1
.
Then
\mathrm{Sus}(\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sus}(\mathcal{F})
induced by
i
is an equvalence.

Proof.

Since
p_{*}p^{*}
is a t-exact functor, the diagram above induces the same diagram replacing
\mathcal{F}
by

\pi_{m}\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
by
\pi_{m}\mathcal{G}
for any
m\geq 0
.
We will show that the map
\mathrm{Sus}(\pi_{m}\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sus}(\pi_{m}\mathcal{F})
induced by
i
is an equivalence.
For this, it suffices to show that
\mathrm{Sus}(\pi_{m}\mathcal{G})(T)\rightarrow\mathrm{Sus}(\pi_{m}\mathcal{F})(T)
is a homotopy equivalence for any
T\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
,
which follows by the following claim.

Claim
. —

In the statement of the lemma, assume that
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
are presheaves of sets.
Then there exists a simplicial homotopy
\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{F})(T)\times\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{F})(T)

between the identity and
i\circ r
.

To construct a simplicial homotopy for a simplicial set
K
, we must construct a family of maps

h_{m}^{j}\colon K_{m}\rightarrow K_{m+1}
for
m\geq 0
,
m\geq j\geq 0
satisfying the usual compatibility conditions with degeneracy and face maps.
The
k
-th vertex of
\Delta^{m}_{\mathrm{geom}}
is denoted by
v^{m}_{k}
.
We define the map
h_{m}^{j}
as the composition

\mathrm{Sus}_{m}(\mathcal{F})(T)\cong\mathcal{F}(T\times\Delta^{m}_{\mathrm{geom}})\xrightarrow{c}\mathcal{F}(T\times\Delta^{m}_{\mathrm{geom}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\xrightarrow[\sim]{(a^{j}_{m})^{*}}\mathcal{F}(T\times\Delta^{m+1}_{\mathrm{geom}})\cong\mathrm{Sus}_{m+1}(\mathcal{F})(T),

where
a^{j}_{m}
is the affine morphism
\Delta^{m+1}_{\mathrm{geom}}\rightarrow\Delta^{m}_{\mathrm{geom}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}

sending
v^{m+1}_{k}
to
(v^{m}_{k},0)
if
k\leq j
and to
(v^{m}_{k-1},1)
if
k>j
.
It is not hard to check that this is the desired homotopy, and the claim follows.

Now, by the argument of
[
HA
, right after Remark 1.2.4.4]
, the spectral sequence

\mathrm{E}^{2}_{p,q}=\pi_{p}\mathrm{Sus}(\pi_{q}\mathcal{G})\Rightarrow\pi_{p+q}\mathrm{Sus}(\mathcal{G})
converges
(in the sense explained in the proof of
[
HA
, 1.2.2.14]
).
We have a similar spectral sequence for
\mathcal{F}
and they are compatible with respect to
i
.
Since the map
\pi_{p}\mathrm{Sus}(\pi_{q}\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\pi_{p}\mathrm{Sus}(\pi_{q}\mathcal{F})
iduced by
i
is an equivalence,
the map
\pi_{m}\mathrm{Sus}(\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\pi_{m}\mathrm{Sus}(\mathcal{F})
is equivalence for any
m\geq 0
.
Thus, the claim follows.
∎

1.17

Let
(\mathscr{S},\mathcal{E},\mu)
be a pre-localization system.
Recall that we have a subsheaf
\mathrm{Adm}_{d}\subset\mathscr{S}_{d}\otimes\mu^{\infty}
from
Definition
.
By construction,
\mathrm{Adm}_{d-1}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Adm}_{d}
, thus
\mathrm{Adm}_{d}
is organized into a filtered (
1
-)sheaf

\mathrm{Adm}\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}}_{\mathrm{Ab}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S})
.
For any
\infty
-presheaf
\mathcal{F}
, we have the morphism
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sus}_{m}(\mathcal{F})

induced by the projection
\Delta^{m}_{\mathrm{geom}}\rightarrow S
, which admits a retraction defined by any rational point of
\Delta^{m}_{\mathrm{geom}}
.
In particular, we have
\mathscr{S}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sus}_{m}(\mathscr{S})
.
This enables us to define

\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}:=\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Adm})\cap(\mathscr{S}\otimes\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mu^{\infty})),

where the intersection is taken in
\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{S}\otimes\mu^{\infty})
,
and which belongs to the abelian category
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Ab}}^{\mathrm{fil}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}))
.
The map
\mathscr{S}\otimes\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mu^{\infty})\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
, induced by the unique map
\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mu^{\infty})\rightarrow\Delta^{0}
,
induces the morphism
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
.
We denote the value of
d\in\mathrm{N}(\mathbb{N})
in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Ab}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}))

of
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
by
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{d,\bullet}
.
More concretely, for
T\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
,

\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{d,m}(T)=\mathrm{Adm}_{d}(T\times\Delta^{m}_{\mathrm{geom}})\cap\bigl(\mathscr{S}(T)\otimes\mu^{\infty}(T\times\Delta^{m}_{\mathrm{geom}})\bigr).

The geometric realization of
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
, namely
\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
,
is denoted by
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}
, defined in
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S})
.
We note that the construction is functorial and we have the functor

\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\colon\mathrm{LSys}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Ab}}^{\mathrm{fil}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}))
.
The following contractibility of
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}
is crucial for us.

Lemma
. —

If the pre-localization system
(\mathscr{S},\mathcal{E},\mu)
is a localization system,
the canonical morphism
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{cdh}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{cdh}}

is an equivalence in
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
.

Proof.

Since equivalence of a morphism of functors can be checked pointwise (by
[
HTT
, 3.1.3.1]
),
it suffices to show the equivalence of the morphism
(\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{d})_{\mathrm{cdh}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}_{d,\mathrm{cdh}}

of
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
-valued
\infty
-sheaves for each
d\geq 0
.
Since the verification is local with respect to cdh-topology, we may fix a point
z
of
S_{\mathrm{cdh}}
,
and show that the morphism of stalks
(\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{d})_{z}\rightarrow(\mathscr{S}_{d})_{z}
is a homotopy equivalence.
For this, it suffices to show that the map of simplicial sets
(\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{d,\bullet})_{z}\rightarrow(\mathscr{S}_{d})_{z}

is a trivial fibration of simplicial sets by
[
SAG
, A.5.3.7]
.
Since
(\mathscr{S}_{d})_{z}
is a discrete simplicial set, it suffices to show that each fiber of
(\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{d,\bullet})_{z}
is Kan contractible.
Since
(\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{d,\bullet})_{z}
is an abelian simplicial set, it is a Kan complex.
Let
A_{\bullet}
be the fiber over a vertex
F\in(\mathscr{S}_{d})_{z}
.
Since
(\mathscr{S}_{d})_{z}
is discrete,
A_{\bullet}
is a retract of the Kan compex
(\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{d,\bullet})_{z}
,
so
A_{\bullet}
is a Kan complex as well.
We must solve the following lifting problem for
n\geq 0
:

(
\star
)

\textstyle{\partial\Delta^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{A_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta^{0}.}

There exists
T\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
around
z
so that the map
a
corresponds to
n+1
elements of

\mathscr{S}(T)\otimes\mu^{l}(T\times\Delta^{n-1}_{\mathrm{geom}})
for large enough
l
.
Since
\mu
admits a contraction morphism
c_{\mu}
, the Kan fibration

\mathscr{S}(T)\otimes\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mu^{l})(T)\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(T)
is a trivial fibration by Lemma
1.16
.
In particular, we can find a map
\Delta^{n}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(T)\otimes\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mu^{l})(T)
whose boundary is equal to
a
.
Let
\sum(F_{i};\mathbf{f}_{i})
be the corresponding element of
\mathscr{S}(T)\otimes\mu^{l}(T\times\Delta^{n}_{\mathrm{geom}})
.
Note that
\sum F_{i}=F
.
By Remark
Definition
.
2
, by shrinking
T
around
z
, we may find
\mathbf{g}\in\mu^{l^{\prime}}(T)
such that

(F_{i};\mathbf{f}_{i}\vee\mathbf{g})
belongs to
\mathrm{Adm}(T\times\Delta^{n}_{\mathrm{geom}})
for any
i
.
This element can be identified with a map
\widetilde{b}\colon\Delta^{n}\rightarrow A_{\bullet}
.
Now, let
B_{k}:=\Delta^{[n]\setminus\{k\}}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}
be the face,
and
\phi_{k}\colon\Delta^{n-1}_{\mathrm{geom}}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}_{\mathrm{geom}}
be the closed immersion corresponding to
B_{k}
.
We may write the element corresponding to
a|_{B_{k}}
as
\sum(G^{(k)}_{j};\mathbf{h}^{(k)}_{j})

so that
(G^{(k)}_{j};\mathbf{h}^{(k)}_{j})\in\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{d,n-1}
.
By the choice of
\sum(F_{i};\mathbf{f}_{i})
, we have
\sum(F_{i};\phi_{k}^{*}\mathbf{f}_{i})=\sum(G^{(k)}_{j};\mathbf{h}^{(k)}_{j})
.
The map
\widetilde{b}|_{B_{k}}
corresponds to

(
\star\star
)

\sum\phi_{k}^{*}(F_{i};\mathbf{f}_{i}\vee\mathbf{g})=\sum(F_{i};\phi_{k}^{*}(\mathbf{f}_{i})\vee\mathbf{g})=\sum(G^{(k)}_{j};\mathbf{h}^{(k)}_{j}\vee\mathbf{g}),

where the first equality holds since
\mathbf{g}
is in
\mu^{\infty}(T)
instead of
\mu^{\infty}(T\times\Delta^{n}_{\mathrm{geom}})
.
Now, the contraction morphism induces the map
\Delta^{1}\times B_{k}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(T)\otimes\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mu^{\infty})(T)

defined by
\sum(G^{(k)}_{j};\mathbf{h}^{(k)}_{j}\vee c_{\mu}(\mathbf{g}))
(where
c_{\mu}
is the contraction morphism).
Since
(G^{(k)}_{j},\mathbf{h}^{(k)}_{j})
already belongs to
\mathrm{Adm}_{d}
, this map factors through
A_{\bullet}
.
By construction, this map yields
c\colon\Delta^{1}\times\partial\Delta^{n}\rightarrow A_{\bullet}
.
Finally, consider the following diagram:

\textstyle{\Delta^{1}\times\partial\Delta^{n}\coprod_{\{1\}\times\partial\Delta^{n}}\{1\}\times\Delta^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c\coprod\widetilde{b}}
\textstyle{A_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta^{0},}

where
c\coprod\widetilde{b}
is well-defined by (

⋆
⁣
⋆

‣
1.17
).
Since the left vertical inclusion is anodyne by
[
HTT
, 2.1.2.7]
and
A_{\bullet}
is a Kan complex,
the lifting problem has a solution.
The restriction to
\{0\}\times\Delta^{n}
yields the solution to the lifting problem (

⋆

‣
1.17
).
∎

1.18 Lemma
. —

Let
n>0
,
m\geq 0
be integers.
The inclusion
(\partial\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n}\cup\Delta^{m}\times\Lambda^{n}_{n},e)\rightarrow(\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n},e)
,
where
e
is the union of the edge
\{m\}\times\Delta^{\{n-1,n\}}

(
if this edge belongs to the simplicial set
)

and the set of degenerate edges, is marked anodyne.

Proof.

The case
m=0
follows by
[
HTT
, 3.1.2.3]
.
For
a,b>0
, let
I
be a sequence of integers
\{0\leq i_{1}\leq\dots\leq i_{a}\leq b\}
.
Let
\sigma^{(a,b)}_{I}
be the
(a+b)
-simplex of
\Delta^{a}\times\Delta^{b}
whose vertices are

(0,0),\dots,(0,i_{1}),(1,i_{1}),\dots,(1,i_{2}),(2,i_{2}),\dots,(a,b)
.
Let
K\subset\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n-1}
be the simplicial subset spanned by all non-degenerate simplex except for
the
(m+n-1)
-simplex
\sigma^{(m,n-1)}_{\{0,\dots,0\}}
.
We view
\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n-1}
as a simplicial subset of
\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n}
via the canonical inclusion

\Delta^{n-1}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}
(that avoids the vertex
n\in\Delta^{n}
).
Then
K_{0}:=K\cap(\partial\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n}\cup\Delta^{m}\times\Lambda^{n}_{n})\rightarrow K
is an inner anodyne map.
Indeed,

\displaystyle K_{0}\subset K_{0}\cup(\sigma_{\{n-1,\dots,n-1\}})

\displaystyle\subset K_{0}\cup(\sigma_{\{n-1,\dots\}}\cup\sigma_{\{n-2,n-1,\dots\}})

\displaystyle\subset\dots\subset K_{0}\cup(\sigma_{\{n-1,\dots\}}\cup\dots\sigma_{\{0,n-1\dots\}})=:K_{1},

where
\sigma_{I}:=\sigma^{(m,n-1)}_{I}
, is a sequence of inner anodyne maps.
Then we proceed as
K_{1}\subset K_{1}\cup\sigma_{\{0,n-2,n-1,\dots\}}\subset K_{1}\cup(\sigma_{\{0,n-2,n-1,\dots\}}\cup\sigma_{\{0,n-3,n-1,\dots\}})
.
By repeating this procedure, we get the inner anodyne inclusion
K_{0}\hookrightarrow K
.

Now, put
L_{0}:=(K\cup\partial\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n}\cup\Delta^{m}\times\Lambda^{n}_{n},e)
.
Since
L_{0}=(\partial\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n}\cup\Delta^{m}\times\Lambda^{n}_{n},e)\coprod_{K_{0}}K
,
it remains to show that
L_{0}\rightarrow(\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n},e)

is a marked anodyne map.
Let
L\subset\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n}
be the simplicial subset spanned by all the non-degenerate simplices except for
the
(m+n)
-simplex
\sigma^{(m,n)}_{\{0,\dots,0\}}
.
We proceed exactly as above and we have inner anodyne
L_{0}\subset L
.
Finally,
(L,e)\subset(\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n},e)
is a marked anodyne map.
Indeed, we have the following diagram

\textstyle{\Lambda^{n+m}_{n+m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta^{n+m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{(m,n)}_{\{0,\dots,0\}}}
\textstyle{\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n}.}

Since this diagram is pushout as well, we get the claim by
[
HTT
, 3.1.1.1 (2)]
.
∎

1.19 Corollary
. —

Let
n>0
be an integer.
Let
p\colon X\rightarrow\Delta^{1}
be a map of simplicial sets, and let
X_{0}:=p^{-1}(0)
,
X_{1}:=p^{-1}(1)
.
The the inclusion
\bigl(X\times\Lambda^{n}_{n},\mathcal{E}\cap(X\times\Lambda^{n}_{n})\bigr)\cup(X_{0}\times\Delta^{n})^{\flat}\hookrightarrow(X\times\Delta^{n},\mathcal{E})
,
where
\mathcal{E}
is the union of the set of edges of the form
\{x\}\times\Delta^{\{n-1,n\}}
for a vertex
x
of
X_{1}
and the set of degenerate edges,
is marked anodyne.

Proof.

First, if
X=X_{0}\coprod X_{1}
, then the claim follows by
[
HTT
, 3.1.2.3]
.
Thus, by working simplex by simplex, it suffices to show the following claim:
Let
q\colon Y\rightarrow\Delta^{1}
be a map of simplicial sets and

f\colon\partial\Delta^{m}\rightarrow Y
be a map such that
m>0
and
f(m)\in Y_{1}:=q^{-1}(1)
.
Let
Y\hookrightarrow Y^{\prime}:=Y\coprod_{f,\partial\Delta^{m}}\Delta^{m}
be the inclusion.
Let
\mathcal{E}_{Y}
be the set of edges of
Y\times\Delta^{n}
which is either degenerate edge or
edge of the form
\{y\}\times\Delta^{\{n,n-1\}}
for a vertex
y
of
Y_{1}
.
Then the map
\bigl(Y\times\Delta^{n}\cup Y^{\prime}\times\Lambda^{n}_{n},\mathcal{E}_{Y})\hookrightarrow(Y^{\prime}\times\Delta^{n},\mathcal{E}_{Y})

is marked anodyne.
This follows by Lemma
1.18
.
∎

1.20
Proof of Theorem
1.11

We define a simplicial set
C_{\mathcal{X}}:=\mathrm{LSys}\coprod_{\mathcal{X}\times\{0\}}(\mathcal{X}\times\Delta^{1})\coprod_{\mathcal{X}\times\{1\}}\mathrm{Coef}
.
By small object argument, we have a factorization
C_{\mathcal{X}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}

where the first inclusion is inner anodyne and the second map is an inner fibration.
The map
\mathcal{X}\times\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{PreLSys}^{\dagger})\times\Delta^{1}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ev}}\mathrm{PreLSys}^{\dagger}
,
where
\mathrm{ev}
is the evaluation map, induces the map
C_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathrm{PreLSys}^{\dagger}
.
We will construct a functor
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{P})
, informally sending
(\mathscr{S},\mathcal{E},\mu)
to
the map
\mathscr{S}\rightarrow(a\,\mathrm{Sus}\,\Gamma^{\infty}_{r_{\infty}}\mathrm{S})^{\infty}\mathscr{S}
, where
a
is the cdh-sheafification functor.

First, let us construct a diagram
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\colon C_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}})
.
We have already constructed
\beta\colon C_{\mathcal{X},0}\times\Delta^{1}\cong\mathrm{LSys}\times\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}})

sending
(\mathscr{S},\mathcal{E},\mu)
to the canonical map
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{S}\times\mu^{\infty})
in
1.17
.
On the other hand, we have the composition

\alpha\colon C_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathrm{PreLSys}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}}\mathrm{Fun}(\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}})
,
where the second functor sends
\mathscr{S}
to
\mathscr{S}\times\mu^{\infty}
.
Combining these, we have the diagram

(C_{\mathcal{X}}\times\Lambda^{1}_{1})\coprod_{C_{\mathcal{X},0}\times\Lambda^{1}_{1}}(C_{\mathcal{X},0}\times\Delta^{1})\xrightarrow{\alpha\sqcup\beta}\mathrm{Fun}(\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}}).

By Corollary
1.19
, this extends to a diagram

\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\colon C_{\mathcal{X}}\times\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}})
.
By construction, for
\mathcal{H}\in\mathrm{Coef}
, we have
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{H})\simeq\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cons}})
.

Let
X:=\Delta^{1}\sqcup_{\Delta^{\{1\}}}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1})
, where the morphism

\Delta^{\{1\}}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}
is determined by
(0,0)\in\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}
.
The diagram (
1.14.i
) yields the following diagram

C_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(X,\mathrm{Fun}(\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}}))
:

(
\star
)

\textstyle{\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}(\mathscr{S}\otimes\mu^{\infty})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}}\Gamma^{n}(\mathscr{S}\otimes\mu^{\infty})\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}}\bm{\partial}^{n}\mathrm{gr}(\mathscr{S}\otimes\mu^{\infty})\bigr)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}}\Gamma^{n}\mathrm{gr}(\mathscr{S}\otimes\mu^{\infty})\bigr),}

where the framed object corresponds to the
(0,1)
-vertex of
\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}
.
Since
X\hookrightarrow(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1})^{\triangleleft}
is an inner anodyne,
the functor extends to

F\colon C_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}\bigl((\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1})^{\triangleleft},\mathrm{Fun}(\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}})\bigr)
.
Consider the composition

\displaystyle\mathrm{LSys}\cong C_{\mathcal{X},0}\xrightarrow{F}\mathrm{Fun}\bigl((\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1})^{\triangleleft},\mathrm{Fun}(\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}})\bigr)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{\{-\infty,(0,1)\}},\mathrm{Fun}(\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}}))

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Fun}\bigl(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Fun}(\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{fil}})\bigr).

By taking adjoint, we have the edge

G\colon\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}\bigl(\mathrm{LSys}\times\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathrm{N}(\mathbb{N}),\mathcal{P}\bigr).

We wish to construct a homotopy between the morphism determined by
G
and
0
.
Recall that we have the standard t-structure on
\mathcal{P}
.
Recall that we are using
homological
convention.
We claim that
G
factors through
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{LSys}\times\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathrm{N}(\mathbb{N}),\mathcal{P}_{\leq 0})
.
Indeed, since
\mathcal{P}_{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}
is fully faithful, the verification is pointwise.
For each
m\geq 0
,
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{m}
is a
1
-presheaf, thus it belongs to
\mathcal{P}_{\leq 0}
.
On the other hand, we have

\pi_{i}\mathrm{Sus}_{m}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits\bm{\partial}^{n}\mathrm{gr}(\mathscr{S}\otimes\mu^{\infty})\bigr)\simeq 0

for
i>0
. Indeed,
\mathrm{gr}(\mathscr{S}\otimes\mu^{\infty})
is a
1
-sheaf,

\mathrm{Sus}_{m}
and
\bm{\partial}^{n}
are left t-exact (where the former is even t-exact),
and
\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits
is t-exact by
[
HA
, 1.3.5.21]
.
Thus, the claim follows.
Now, we have the pair of adjoint functors
\iota\colon\mathcal{P}^{\heartsuit}\leftrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\leq 0}\colon\tau_{\geq 0}
by
[
HA
, 1.2.1.7]
.
Composing the adjunction morphism with
G
, we have a morphism
\iota\circ\tau_{\geq 0}\circ G\rightarrow G
.
This morphism, considered as a diagram

\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{LSys}\times\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathrm{N}(\mathbb{N}),\mathcal{P})
,
can be depicted as

\textstyle{\iota\circ\tau_{\geq 0}\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota\circ\tau_{\geq 0}\circ G}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\iota\circ\tau_{\geq 0}\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits\bm{\partial}^{n}(\mathscr{S}\otimes\mu^{\infty})\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits\bm{\partial}^{n}(\mathscr{S}\otimes\mu^{\infty})\bigr).}

The left vertical edge is an equivalence since
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{m}
is in
\mathcal{P}^{\heartsuit}
for each
m
.
Since, by the very definition of
\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}
, the homomorphism (of abelian presheaves)

\mathrm{Adm}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\rightarrow\pi_{0}\mathrm{Sus}_{\bullet}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits\bm{\partial}^{n}\mathrm{gr}(\mathscr{S}\times\mu^{\infty})\bigr)
is
equal
to
0
,

\tau_{\geq 0}\circ G\simeq\pi_{0}(G)
is
equal
to
0
.
Thus the morphism
\iota\circ\tau_{\geq 0}\circ G
is homotopic to
0
.
Since the left vertical edge is an equivalence, this homotopy induces a digram

.
For this, it suffices to show that the composition of the 1st row and the 3rd row are homotopic to the identity.
We only need to show this for the 1st row, since the 3rd row is a particular case of the 1st row.
Consider the canonical map

).

\alpha
in the above diagram is homotopic to the
H
composed with the restriction to

\{1\}\times\Delta^{1}\subset\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}
.

, the map

yields a homotopy between

and

.
Thus,

is homotopic to the map
\mathrm{Map}(\mathscr{S},\mathcal{H})\xrightarrow{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H})\circ}\mathrm{Map}(\mathscr{S},\bm{\Gamma}\mathcal{H})
.
Since
\mathrm{rw}\circ L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H})\simeq\mathrm{id}
, we get the claim, and the theorem is proven.
∎

2
Construction of rewind morphism

Let
X\rightarrow S
be a morphism of finite type between noetherian separated schemes,
and assume we are given a cohomology theory with “suitable” six functor formalism.
Then we have the “Borel-Moore homology functor”
\underline{\mathrm{H}}^{\mathrm{BM}}_{X/S}\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
for some commutative ring
R
,
informally, sending
T\rightarrow S
to
\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(X_{T}/T)
(see Introduction for the notation).
When the six functor formalism satisfies some properties, it is not hard to check that this functor is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant and a
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-valued cdh-sheaf.
Furthermore, it admits a rewind structure, whose construction is the main theme of this section.
In fact, we define a rewind structure in a more general setting for the later use.

Some
\infty
-categorical constructions

We start with some general non-sense of
\infty
-category theory.

2.1

Let
f\colon T\rightarrow S
be a map of simplicial sets.
We have the functor
f^{*}\colon(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta})_{/S}\rightarrow(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta})_{/T}
sending
X\rightarrow S
to the pullback
X\times_{S}T\rightarrow T
.
As in
[
HTT
, 4.1.2.7]
(4)
(4)

(4)

The description of
p_{*}(M)
in
[
HTT
, 4.1.2.7]
does not seem to be correct.
,

f^{*}
admits a right adjoint
f_{*}
as well as a left adjoint
f_{!}
.
Here,
f_{!}
is simply the functor viewing a simplicial set over
T
as that over
S
via
f
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category, and let
f,g\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}

be the map evaluating at
\Delta^{\{0\}}
and
\Delta^{\{1\}}
respectively.
We define the functor between categories by

\mathcal{S}_{-}:=f_{*}g^{*}\colon(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta})_{/\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta})_{/\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}.

Since both
g^{*}
and
f_{*}
admit left adjoint, the functor
\mathcal{S}_{-}
commutes with small limits.
Let
p\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
be a coCartesian fibration between
\infty
-categories.
We consider the following diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{g^{*}\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta^{\{1\}}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\Delta^{\{0\}}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}.}

Since
f
is a Cartesian fibration by
[
HTT
, 2.4.7.12]
, the map
q
is a coCartesian fibration by
[
HTT
, 3.2.2.12]
.
The fiber of
q
over
X\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
is

\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\{X\},f_{*}g^{*}\mathcal{D})\cong\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}(g_{!}f^{*}\{X\},\mathcal{D})\cong\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\bigl((\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{X/},\mathcal{D}\bigr)

(see
[
HTT
, 4.2.1]
for the definition of the “alternative slice”).

Now, let
\mathcal{D}^{\natural}
be the marked simplicial set with underlying simplicial set
\mathcal{D}
whose marked edges are
p
-coCartesian edges
(cf. dual version of
[
HTT
, 3.1.1.8]
).
We have the maps

\mathcal{D}\xleftarrow{\Delta^{\{0\}}}\mathrm{Map}^{\flat}\bigl((\Delta^{1})^{\sharp},\mathcal{D}^{\natural}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\Delta^{\{1\}}}\mathcal{D}.

The first arrow is a categorical equivalence by
[
HTT
, 3.1.2.3]
since
\Delta^{0}\rightarrow(\Delta^{1})^{\sharp}
is a (dual version of) marked anodyne.
By
[
HTT
, 3.1.2.3]
again,
\mathrm{Map}^{\flat}(\dots)
is an
\infty
-category.
By choosing a quasi-inverse of the first map, we have the map
f^{*}\mathcal{D}\rightarrow g^{*}\mathcal{D}
,
and induces the functor
e_{\mathcal{D}}\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Informally, this functor sends
D\in\mathcal{D}
to the functor
(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{p(D)/}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
sending
f\colon p(D)\rightarrow Y
to

D_{Y}
such that
D\rightarrow D_{Y}
is a coCartesian edge over
f
.
Assume we are given a map
\alpha\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
of coCartesian fibrations over
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}

which preserves coCartesian edges
.
Because
\alpha
preserves coCartesian edges, we have the map

\mathrm{Map}^{\flat}((\Delta^{1})^{\sharp},\mathcal{D}^{\natural})\rightarrow\mathrm{Map}^{\flat}((\Delta^{1})^{\sharp},\mathcal{D}^{\prime\natural})
.
Thus, we have the equivalence of functors
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\circ e_{\mathcal{D}}\simeq e_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\circ\alpha
.

On the other hand, we have the map
\iota\colon\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})
defined by the unique map
\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\Delta^{0}
.
This induces the functor
\mathcal{S}_{-}:=f_{*}g^{*}\rightarrow f_{*}\iota_{*}\iota^{*}g^{*}\cong\mathrm{id}
.
Thus, we have the functor
\Gamma_{\mathcal{D}}\colon\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
.
Informally, this sends a functor
F\colon(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{X/}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
to the “global section”
F(X)
.
Unwinding the definition, we have
\Gamma_{\mathcal{D}}\circ e_{\mathcal{D}}\simeq\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{D}}
.

If we are given a functor
F\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
of coCartesian fibrations over
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
, we define

\underline{F}\colon\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{e_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{S}_{F}}\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}.

Since
\Gamma_{\mathcal{E}}\circ e_{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}
and by the functoriality of
\Gamma_{-}
,
we have an equivalence
\Gamma_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\underline{F}\simeq F
.
Furthermore, if we are given another functor
G\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathcal{S}_{G}\circ\mathcal{S}_{F}=\mathcal{S}_{G\circ F}
, and thus

\mathcal{S}_{G}\circ\underline{F}=\underline{G\circ F}
.

Lemma
. —

For a functor
F\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
between coCartesian fibrations over
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}

(
which may not preserve coCartesian edges
)
,
the induced functor
\underline{F}
preserves coCartesian edges.

Proof.

Let us describe coCartesian edges of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Let
e\colon X\rightarrow Y
be an edge in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
, and view
\Delta^{1}
as a simplicial set over
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
by
e
.
By definition we have

\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}})\cong\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})}(f^{*}\Delta^{1},g^{*}\mathcal{D})
,
thus, an edge of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
corresponds to a map

A\colon f^{*}\Delta^{1}\rightarrow g^{*}\mathcal{D}
over
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})
.
The set of edges of
f^{*}\Delta^{1}
can be identified with the set of diagrams
\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
of the form

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\scriptstyle{D}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{W.}

Consider this diagram as an edge
(X\rightarrow Z)\rightarrow(Y\rightarrow W)
of
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})
.
This edge is
f
-Cartesian if and only if the edge
\alpha
is an equivalence by
[
HTT
, 2.4.7.12]
.
By
[
HTT
, 3.2.2.12]
, the edge of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
corresponding to
A
is a
q
-coCartesian edge
if and only if for any diagram
D
above such that
\alpha
is an equivalence the edge
(\mathrm{proj}\circ A)(D)
,
where
\mathrm{proj}\colon g^{*}\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is the projection, in
\mathcal{D}
over
Z\rightarrow W
is
p
-coCartesian.

Now, let
e\colon X\rightarrow Y
be an edge of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
, and let
E_{e}\colon E_{X}\rightarrow E_{Y}
be a coCartesian edge of
\mathcal{E}
over
e
.
Then the functor
f^{*}\Delta^{1}\rightarrow g^{*}\mathcal{E}
corresponding to
e_{\mathcal{E}}(E_{e})
sends
D
above to the diagram on the left below in
\mathcal{E}
:

\textstyle{E_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E_{e}}
\textstyle{E_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{E_{W},}

\textstyle{F(E_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F(E_{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F(E_{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F(E_{\alpha})}
\textstyle{F(E_{W}).}

Here the
\rightsquigarrow
means that the edge is coCartesian.
By
[
HTT
, 2.4.1.7]
,
E_{\alpha}
is a coCartesian edge as well.
Now, assume that
D
is
f
-Cartesian, or equivalently,
\alpha
is an equivalent edge.
Then,
E_{\alpha}
is equivalent by
[
HTT
, 2.4.1.5]
.
Now, the functor
f^{*}\Delta^{1}\rightarrow g^{*}\mathcal{D}
corresponding to the edge
\underline{F}(E_{e})

sends an edge
D
of
f^{*}\Delta^{1}
to the diagram on the right above viewed as an edge of
g^{*}\mathcal{D}
.
Since
E_{\alpha}
is an equivalence,
F(E_{\alpha})
is an equivalence,
thus it is a coCartesian edge in
\mathcal{D}
over
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
by
[
HTT
, 2.4.1.5]
once again and the characterization of
q
-coCartesian edges.
This implies that the edge
\underline{F}(E_{e})
is a coCartesian edge as required.
∎

Example
. —

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category and take
p
to be the projection
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
The fiber of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
over
X\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
is nothing but
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathcal{C}^{/X})\simeq\mathrm{Fun}((\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{X/},\mathcal{E})
.
Assume we are given a morphism
h\colon Y\rightarrow X
in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{F}_{Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathcal{C}^{/Z})
for
Z\in\{X,Y\}
.
Let
h^{*}\colon\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathcal{C}^{/X})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathcal{C}^{/Y})
be the “pullback functor”.
The proof of the previous lemma implies that giving an edge
\mathcal{F}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{Y}
in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}

is equivalent to giving a morphism
h^{*}\mathcal{F}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{Y}
,
and the edge is coCartesian if and only if the morphism is an equivalence.

2.2

We will also use a variant of the construction of the previous paragraph.
Keeping the notations, we put
\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}:=f_{*}f^{*}\mathcal{D}
.
This is also a coCartesian fibration over
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
,
and have the functor
\Gamma^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\colon\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
.
We have the natural transform
f\Rightarrow g
of functors.
If
p
is at the same time a Cartesian fibration, this natural transform induces the functor

P_{\mathcal{D}}\colon\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
as in
1.4
.
This map does
not
preserve coCartesian edges in general.
Assume we are given a functor
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
of coCartesian fibrations over
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
We denote
P_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\underline{F}
by
\underline{F}^{\star}
.
We have
\Gamma^{\star}\circ\underline{F}^{\star}\simeq F
.
Finally, let
\pi\colon\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\Delta^{0}
be the unique map.
For any
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, we have

\mathcal{S}_{\pi^{*}\mathcal{D}_{0}}\cong\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\pi^{*}\mathcal{D}_{0}}
.

Example
. —

A vertex of
\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
over
X\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
is nothing but
\mathrm{Fun}((\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{X/},\mathcal{D}_{X})
.
Using this presentation, informally,
P_{\mathcal{D}}
sends a functor
F\colon(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{X/}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
to a functor

F^{\prime}\colon(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{X/}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X}
such that
F^{\prime}(X\rightarrow Y)
is a
p
-Cartesian pullback of
F(X\rightarrow Y)
over
X\rightarrow Y
.
Assume we are given an edge
h\colon X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Given
F\in\mathrm{Fun}((\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{X/},\mathcal{D}_{X})
, the coCartesian pushforward of

F\in(\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}})_{X}
by
h
is the composition
h^{*}\circ F\circ\iota_{h}
,
where
\iota_{h}\colon(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{Y/}\rightarrow(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{X/}
, and
h^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{Y}

is the functor associated to the coCartesian fibration
p
.
This can be shown similarly to Lemma
2.1
.

2.3

Let
\pi
be the unique map
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\Delta^{0}
.
Let
\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
be a presentable fibration (cf.
[
HTT
, 5.5.3.2]
) and
\mathcal{A}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Assume we are given a functor
t\colon\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
of coCartesian fibrations over

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
which preserves coCartesian edges.
We also assume that for each
c\in\mathcal{C}
, the fiber
t_{c}
commutes with colimits separately in each variable.
Since the functor
\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{-}
commutes with products, we have the functor

t^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{S}_{\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}}\times_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
.
The fiber of
t^{\prime}
over
c\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
is the functor

\mathrm{Fun}\bigl((\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{c/},\mathcal{A}\bigr)^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathrm{Fun}\bigl((\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{c/},\mathcal{D}_{c}\bigr)\cong\mathrm{Fun}\bigl((\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{c/},\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{D}_{c}\bigr)\xrightarrow{t_{c}}\mathrm{Fun}\bigl((\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{c/},\mathcal{D}_{c}\bigr).

This is nothing but the pointwise left-tensor functor

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{C}^{/c})\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{c}}(\mathcal{C}^{/c})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{c}}(\mathcal{C}^{/c})

(cf.
A.4
or more precisely
[
HA
, 2.1.3.4]
).
The functor
t^{\prime}
preserves coCartesian edges since the left-tensor is pointwise,
t
is assumed to preserve coCartesian edges,
and by the description of coCartesian edges in Example
2.2
.
Since the left-tensor functor
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{D}_{c}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{c}
commutes with small colimits separately in each variable,
so is
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{C}^{/c})\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{c}}(\mathcal{C}^{/c})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{c}}(\mathcal{C}^{/c})
.
Since
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{C}^{/c})
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{c}}(\mathcal{C}^{/c})
are presentable,

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{C}^{/c})
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{c}}(\mathcal{C}^{/c})
admit cotensor and morphism objects by
[
HA
, 4.2.1.33]
.
Thus, by
B.2
, we have the morphism object functor and the cotensor functor

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\times_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}},\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}}\times_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}.

For the later use, we further assume the following:

The
\infty
-category
\mathcal{A}
is compactly generated and endowed with symmetric monoidal structure,
for each
c\in\mathcal{C}
, the functor
t_{c}
can be promoted to a left-tensor structure,
and the fiber
\mathcal{D}_{c}
is compactly generated.

In other words, (*) of
A.4
is satisfied fiberwise.
Moreover, note that
B.4
is also satisfied for
t
.

2.4

Let
\mathcal{D}
,
\pi
be as in the previous paragraph.
Let
p\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be a Cartesian
\infty
-site (cf.
A.7
).
Assume we are given a functor
F\colon\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
over
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
For each
T\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
, we have the induced functor between the fibers

\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}}_{T}\xrightarrow{F\times_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\{T\}}(\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}})_{T}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{T}}(\mathcal{C}_{/T}).

We often denote this composition by
F_{T}
.

Lemma
. —

Assume we are given a Grothendieck topology
\tau
on
\mathcal{C}
and
F
satisfies the following conditions:

•

For each
E\in\mathcal{E}_{T}
, the
\mathcal{D}_{T}
-valued presheaf
F(E)\in(\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}})_{T}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{T}}(\mathcal{C}_{/T})

is contained in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}_{T}}(\mathcal{C}_{/T})
with respect to the topology on
\mathcal{C}_{/T}
induced from
(\mathcal{C},\tau)
;

•

The induced functor
F_{T}\colon\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}}_{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}_{T}}(\mathcal{C}_{/T})

is a
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}_{T}}(\mathcal{C}_{/T})
-valued sheaf on
\mathcal{E}_{T}
.

Then the induced functor
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\xrightarrow{\Gamma^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}}\mathcal{D}

belongs to
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{E})

(
cf.
A.8
)
.

Proof.

We denote the functor
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
, viewed as an object of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{E})
, by
G
.
By Lemma
A.8
, it suffices to show that the composition

\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}}_{T}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{G}\mathcal{D}
, which factors through
\mathcal{D}_{T}
, is a
\mathcal{D}_{T}
-valued sheaf on
\mathcal{E}_{T}
.
This composition is homotopic to the composition
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}}_{T}\xrightarrow{F_{T}}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}_{T}}(\mathcal{C}_{/T})\xrightarrow{\Gamma}\mathcal{D}_{T}
,
where
\Gamma
is the global section functor.
Since
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}_{T}}(\mathcal{C}_{/T})
is presentable and
F_{T}
is a
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}_{T}}(\mathcal{C}_{/T})
-valued sheaf,
F_{T}
extends to
a limit preserving functor
\widetilde{F}_{T}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{E}_{T})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}_{T}}(\mathcal{C}_{/T})
by
[
SAG
, 1.3.1.7]
.
The composition is equivalent to the composition

\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}}_{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{E}_{T})^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{F}_{T}}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{C}_{/T})\xrightarrow{\Gamma}\mathcal{D}.

Since
\Gamma
is limit preserving by Lemma
A.3
,
\Gamma\circ\widetilde{F}_{T}
is limit preserving as well.
Invoking
[
SAG
, 1.3.1.7]
again, we conclude.
∎

Suslin-Voevodsky’s cycle group

We introduce the relative cycle group
z(X/S)
of Suslin-Voevodsky.
By the assignment
z(X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime})
to
S^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
, it becomes a cdh-sheaf.
On the other hand, even though we have a functoriality with respect to proper pushforward of
X
,
the sheaf theoretic behavior of
z(X/S)
with respect to this pushforward is not so well.
This causes difficulties in our construction of rewind structure.
We remedy this by using the categorical preparations we have made so far.
From now on, we fix a commutative ring
R
, and
S
denotes a separated noetherian scheme of finite Krull dimension.

2.5

Let
\mathbf{P}
be the class of morphisms in
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
which contains all the isomorphisms and which is stable under compositions and pullbacks.
Let
\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathbf{P}}_{S}
be the subcategory of
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Sch}_{/S})
with the same objects
and a morphism
(X\rightarrow T)\rightarrow(X^{\prime}\rightarrow T^{\prime})
belongs to
\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathbf{P}}_{S}
if it can be written as

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\times_{T^{\prime}}T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{\prime}}

where
\alpha
belongs to
\mathbf{P}
.
The object
X\rightarrow T
in
\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathbf{P}}_{S}
is also denoted by
X/T
.

Now, we abbreviate
\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{S}
by
\mathrm{Ar}_{S}
or
\mathrm{Ar}
, where
\mathrm{prop}
is the class of proper morphisms.
This category admits fiber products, and can be computed pointwise.
The evaluation at
\{1\}\in\Delta^{1}
induces the functor
\mathrm{Ar}_{S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
This is a Cartesian fibration by
[
HTT
, 6.1.1.1]
.
Let
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{prop}}
be the subcategory of
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
consisting of the same objects and a morphism

T\rightarrow T^{\prime}
over
S
is a morphism in
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{/S}
if it is proper.
The fiber of the Cartesian fibration
\mathrm{Ar}_{S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
over
T\rightarrow S
is the category
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{/T}
.
Thus, by taking the dual construction (cf.
B.1
),
we have another Cartesian fibration
\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}_{S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}

whose fiber over
T\rightarrow S
is, now,
(\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{/T})^{\mathrm{op}}
.

2.6

Let
X\rightarrow S
be a morphism of noetherian schemes of finite type, and
n\geq 0
is an integer.
Suslin-Voevodsky defined an abelian group
\Gamma(S,z(X/S,n))
in
[
SV
, §3.3]
.
In this paper, we denote
\Gamma(S,z(X/S,n))
simply by
z(X/S,n)
,
and
z(X/S,n)
does
not
mean the presheaf contrary to the notation of
[
SV
]
.

For
(Y/T)\in\mathrm{Ar}
, let
\iota_{Y/T}\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/T}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}
be the functor sending
T^{\prime}\rightarrow T
to
Y_{T^{\prime}}/T^{\prime}
.
In
[
A2
, 5.1, 5.2]
, we introduced a topology on
\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}
so that we have the following diagram of geometric morphisms of
\infty
-topoi:

\textstyle{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/T,\mathrm{cdh}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\mathrm{s}}_{Y/T}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{Y/T}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}).}

Here, the vertical morphisms are the “topological localizations”.
We also showed that
(\iota^{\mathrm{s}}_{Y/T})^{*}
coincides with the composition
\circ\iota_{Y/T}
.
Now, by associating
z(Y/T,n)_{R}:=z(Y/T,n)\otimes R
to
Y\rightarrow T
, we have a functor

z^{\mathrm{SV}}(n)\colon\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.
This is an abelian sheaf with respect to the topology on
\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}
.
Indeed, we must show that the map
z^{\mathrm{SV}}(n)\rightarrow Lz^{\mathrm{SV}}(n)
is an equivalence of abelian presheaves.
Since equivalences can be checked pointwise, the observation above implies that we only need to show that
\iota^{*}_{Y/T}z^{\mathrm{SV}}(n)
is a cdh-sheaf on
\mathrm{Sch}_{/T}
,
which is exactly the content of
[
SV
, 4.2.9]
.
Now, we define
z(n)
as the sheafification of the
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-valued presheaf

\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{z^{\mathrm{SV}}(n)}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\heartsuit}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.
Arguing similarly to the above, the pullback
(\iota_{Y/T}^{\mathrm{s}})^{*}z(n)
is equivalent to the (
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-valued) sheafification of
\iota^{*}_{Y/T}z^{\mathrm{SV}}(n)

(see also
[
A2
, 5.2]
).
If the integer
n
is obvious, we further simplify
z(n)
by
z
.

2.7

Consider the Cartesian fibration
p\colon\mathrm{Ar}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
evaluating at
\{1\}\in\Delta^{1}
.
This functor commutes with fiber products.
We consider the cdp-topology on the fiber
p^{-1}(T)\simeq\mathrm{Sch}_{/T}
.
Then
p
becomes a Cartesian site.

Now, assume we are in the situation of
2.3
and assume
\mathcal{A}=\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
,
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
We may apply the construction of
2.3
to get a cotensor map

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{\star}_{\pi^{*}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}\times_{\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}}\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
,
and we may apply the construction of
B.4
.
In particular, assume we are given a coCartesian fibration
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
, functors

F\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\pi^{*}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}\cong\mathcal{S}_{\pi^{*}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
,

G\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
,
and a section
m\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Then we have two functors
M_{G}(m)\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{\star}_{\pi^{*}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
and

{}^{F}m\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Note that by Lemma
2.1
,

\underline{z(n)}\colon\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\pi^{*}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
preserves coCartesian edges for any
n
.
We denote
\underline{z(n)}
by
\underline{z}(n)
or
\underline{z}
.
The proof of the following proposition will be given in
2.9
after some preparations.

Proposition
. —

Assume we are given a functor
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
over
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}
.
Recall the notation
\underline{\mathcal{F}}^{\star}:=P_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\underline{\mathcal{F}}\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
.
We assume further that for any
T\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
, the fiber
\underline{\mathcal{F}}^{\star}_{T}
considered as an object of

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{T}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/T})
is a cdh-sheaf.
Then the functor

\Gamma^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ{}^{\underline{z}}\underline{\mathcal{F}}^{\star}\colon\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D},

considered as an object of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}}(\mathrm{Ar})
,
is in fact a
\mathcal{D}
-valued sheaf with respect to the Cartesian site
\mathrm{Ar}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.

Remark
. —

By
2.1
,
\underline{\mathcal{F}}_{T}
,
considered in
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}}(\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/T},\mathcal{D})
, is equivalent to the composition

\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/T}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{D}
.
Unwinding the definition,
(P_{\mathcal{D}}\underline{\mathcal{F}})_{T}
is the
p
-right Kan extension of

\underline{\mathcal{F}}_{T}\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/T}\times\{1\}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}

along the inclusion
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/T}\times\{1\}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/T}\times\Delta^{1}

where the latter
\infty
-category is considered over
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/T}

via the functor sending
T^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/T}
to
T\rightarrow T^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/T})
.
Thus, if
\mathcal{F}
preserves coCartesian edges and if we are in the situation where
\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}

is a coCartesian fibration that comes from a 6 functor formalism as in
2.10
,

\underline{\mathcal{F}}^{\star}_{T}
is a cdh-sheaf by Lemma
C.7
, and we may apply the proposition.

2.8

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a (Grothendieck) site,
and
\mathcal{F}
be a functor
\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
, where
\mathcal{D}
is a presentable
\infty
-category.
Recall that the functor
\mathcal{F}
is said to be a (
\mathcal{D}
-valued
)
cosheaf
(5)
(5)

(5)

For a more general
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{T}
, the
\infty
-category of cosheaf is defined to be
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathcal{T})^{\mathrm{op}}
.
By
[
SAG
, 1.3.1.7]
, this definition is compatible with ours.

if for any
C\in\mathcal{C}
and any covering sieve
\mathrm{R}\rightarrow C
,
the induced morphism
\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(C)
is an equivalence.
The functor
z(n)\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{/T}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is hardly a cosheaf with respect to the cdp-topology on
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{/T}
.
For example, assume that
X\rightarrow T
is a flat morphism of dimension
d
between integral schemes,
and take a cdp-morphism
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
such that
X^{\prime}
is integral and
X^{\prime}\rightarrow T
is
not
equidimensional.
Then we have
z(X/T,d)\cong\mathbb{Z}\cdot[X]
, but
z(X^{\prime}/T,d)=0
.
The issue is that
z(X/T)
behaves well only cdh-locally with respect to
T
.
In the example,
z(X^{\prime}/T)
does possess sections cdh-locally on
T
but not globally.
This issue can be remedied by considering
\underline{z}
instead of
z
.
More precisely, the advantage of considering
\underline{z}
over
z
can be seen from the following lemma:

Lemma
. —

The fiber
\underline{z}_{T}\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{/T}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/T})
of
\underline{z}:=\underline{z}(n)
over
T

factors through the cdh
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/T,\mathrm{cdh}})
,
and the induced functor
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{/T}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/T,\mathrm{cdh}})

is in fact a
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/T,\mathrm{cdh}})
-valued cosheaf with respect to the cdp-topology on
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{/T}
.

Proof.

For
X\rightarrow T\in\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{/T}
, recall that
\iota_{X/T}\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/T}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}

is the functor sending
T^{\prime}\rightarrow T
to
X\times_{T}T^{\prime}\rightarrow T^{\prime}
.
Unwinding the definition,
\underline{z}_{T}(X\rightarrow T)
, viewed as a functor, is equivalent to the composition

(\mathrm{Sch}_{/T})^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{X/T}}\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{z}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}.

By
2.6
, this composition is a
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-valued cdh-sheaf.

Now, we show that
\underline{z}_{T}
is an
\infty
-sheaf-valued cdp-cosheaf.
We abbreviate
\underline{z}_{T}(X\rightarrow T)
by
\underline{z}(X)
.
By
[
SAG
, A.3.3.1]
, it suffices to show that, for a cdp-covering
Y\rightarrow X
,

\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\Delta}\underline{z}(Y_{\bullet})\rightarrow\underline{z}(X)
is an equivalence in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(T_{\mathrm{cdh}})
,
where
T_{\mathrm{cdh}}=\mathrm{Sch}_{/T,\mathrm{cdh}}
.
The
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(T_{\mathrm{cdh}})
has enough points by
[
SAG
, A.4.0.5]
.
By
[
SAG
, 2.1.0.3]
we may check the equivalence as underlying spectra-valued sheaves,
it suffices to show the equivalence around any point of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(T_{\mathrm{cdh}})
.
Since
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(T_{\mathrm{cdh}})
is
1
-localic by
[
HTT
, 6.4.5.6]
, the set of points of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(T_{\mathrm{cdh}})

is isomorphic to that of
T_{\mathrm{cdh}}
.
By
[
GK
, Thm 2.6]
, the points have the following description:
Let
v\colon V\rightarrow T
be a morphism from the spectrum of a henselian valuation ring to
T
.
Then we have the geometric morphism
p_{v}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(T_{\mathrm{cdh}})
such that
p_{v}^{*}(\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{V\rightarrow U}\mathcal{F}(U)
,
where the colimit is taken over the category of morphisms of
T
-schemes
V\rightarrow U
where
U\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/T}
.
Since
p_{v}^{*}
admits a right adjoint
(p_{v})_{*}
, it commutes with arbitrary colimits.
Thus, in order to show the equivalence, it suffices to show the equivalence for

\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits p_{v}^{*}\underline{z}(Y_{\bullet})\rightarrow p_{v}^{*}\underline{z}(X)
.
Recall that
\underline{z}(W)
is the
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-valued sheafification of the
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
-valued presheaf
sending
T^{\prime}\rightarrow T
to
z(W\times_{T}T^{\prime}/T^{\prime},n)_{R}
by construction of
2.6
.
Considering the geometric morphisms
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}\xrightarrow{p_{v}}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(T_{\mathrm{cdh}})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/T})
,
we get that
p^{*}_{v}\underline{z}(W)\simeq\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{V\rightarrow U}z(W\times_{T}U/U,n)_{R}
, where
U\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/T}
and the colimit is taken in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.
For a
V
-scheme
Z
of finite type, denote by
z(Z/V,n)
the free abelian group generated by dimension
n
points of
Z_{\eta}
, where
\eta\in V
is the generic point.
Then we have
\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{V\rightarrow U}z(W\times_{T}U/U,n)_{R}\cong z(W_{V}/V,n)_{R}
.
Indeed, since
\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{V\rightarrow U}
is filtered, both sides are in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
by
[
HA
, 7.1.1.13]
,
and we only need to compute the colimit in the category of abelian groups.
This follows by
[
SV
, 4.2.11]
together with [EGA IV, 11.2.6] (or Gruson-Raynaud’s flattening theorem if one wants)
and the fact that any irreducible
V
-scheme which covers
\eta
is flat.

Now, it remains to check that for a scheme
X
separated of finite type over
V
and a cdp-morphism
Y\rightarrow X
over
V
, the morphism (in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
)

\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\Delta}z(Y_{\bullet}/V,n)_{R}\rightarrow z(X/V,n)_{R}
, where
Y_{m}:=Y\times_{V}\dots\times_{V}Y
(
m+1
-times product), is an equivalence.
For each point
x\in X_{\eta}
of dimension
n
, we may take
y_{x}\in Y
whose residue field does not extend by the definition of cdp-morphism.
Let
y^{(m)}_{x}\in Y_{m}
be the image of the morphism
y_{x}\times_{x}y_{x}\times\dots_{x}y_{x}\rightarrow Y_{m}
(where the product is taken
m+1
-times).
This induces a morphism
z(X/V,n)\rightarrow z(Y_{m}/V,n)
sending
\sum a_{x}[x]
to
\sum a_{x}[y^{(m)}_{x}]
.
Invoking
[
HTT
, 6.1.3.16]
, we get the desired equivalence.
∎

Application to cohomology theory

2.10

Now, we assume given an oriented cohomology theory with 6-functor formalism which admits a trace map
such that each stable
\infty
-category is compactly generated over a noetherian separated scheme
S
of finite Krull dimension.
For a detailed explanation of the terminologies, we refer to §
C
.
The main example we are having in mind is the
\infty
-enhancement of Voevodsky’s theory of motives (cf. Example
C.1
).
Given such a theory we have the coCartesian fibration
p\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
whose fiber over
T
is the
\mathcal{D}_{T}
,
and the functor associated to a morphism
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
is the “normal pullback”
f^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}_{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X}
.
This map is Cartesian as well, since
f^{*}
admits a right adjoint
f_{*}
.
An important feature of the theory is that we may apply the constructions of
B.4
(cf.
C.1
).

In
C.2
, we recalled the “compactly supported cohomology” functor

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
of coCartesian fibrations over
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}
.
Informally, this functor sends
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(X/Y):=f_{!}f^{*}R_{Y}
in
\mathcal{D}_{Y}
,
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}
preserves coCartesian edges.
Consider the functor
\mathrm{id}\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}
sending
T\rightarrow S
to
\mathrm{id}_{T}\colon T\rightarrow T
.
The composition
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}\mathcal{D}
is denoted by
R
.
We believe that there will not be any confusion with the ring
R
we fixed.
We define the bivariant homology functor as

\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}\!\left<n\right>:=M_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(n)[2n]}(R)\colon\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
using the notation of
B.4
,
where
(n)
denotes the
n
-th Tate twist.
The assumption that the theory admits a “trace map” means that we have the trace map
z(d)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}\!\left<d\right>
for any integer
d\geq 0

in
\mathrm{Fun}(\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})
(cf.
C.3
).
By definition of the functor
M_{-}(-)
,
the trace map yields the extranatural transform
z(d)\times\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\!\left<d\right>\xrightarrow[\bullet]{}R
(cf.
B.3
).

2.11

Now, we apply the construction at the beginning of this section to the coCartesian fibration
\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
,
and get a coCartesian fibration
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
From now on,
\pi
is the unique map
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\Delta^{0}
.
Recall that we have the functors

\underline{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\!\left<d\right>\colon\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}},\qquad\underline{z}(d)\colon\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}}}\xrightarrow{z}\mathcal{S}_{\pi^{*}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}.

Informally,
\underline{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\!\left<d\right>(X/Y)\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
sends
Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(X_{Y^{\prime}}/Y^{\prime})\!\left<d\right>
in
\mathcal{D}_{Y^{\prime}}
,
and similarly,
\underline{z}(d)(X/Y)\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/Y}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
sends

Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
to
z(d)(X_{Y^{\prime}}\rightarrow Y^{\prime})
using the notation of
2.6
.
Since
\mathcal{D}
is a
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-linear category, we have the action functor
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\times\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
.
This defines the functor

\underline{z}(d)\times\underline{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\!\left<d\right>\colon\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\times_{\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}.

On the other hand, the section
R\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
induces the section

\underline{R}\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Put
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}
and
\mathcal{E}=\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}
, and consider the following diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{e}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\scriptstyle{\underline{z}\times\underline{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{S}_{R}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{S}_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{S}_{p}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{S}_{z\times\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}}
\textstyle{\Rightarrow}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{D}},}

where
\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}
is introduced in
B.3
.
Since the maps
p
,
q
preserve coCartesian edges, the squares are homotopy commutative by
2.1
.
By functoriality of
\mathcal{S}_{-}
,
the trace map

z(d)\times\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\!\left<d\right>\xrightarrow[\bullet]{}R
in
2.10
induces a natural transform of the lower triangle diagram.
Thus, the diagram yileds the
master trace map

(2.11.i)

\underline{z}(d)\times\underline{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\!\left<d\right>\xrightarrow[\bullet]{}\underline{R}

for any
d\geq 0
.

2.12

We summarize general constructions of cohomological functors.
In order the readers not to be distracted by generalities of cohomology theory, the proofs are postponed till §§
C.8
,
C.9
.

1.

Let
D\rightarrow S
be a morphism, and
q_{D}\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}

be the functor sending
T\rightarrow S
to
T\times_{S}D\rightarrow S
.
Let
f\colon D^{\prime}\rightarrow D
be in
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
We have the functor
Q_{f}^{*}\colon q^{*}_{D}\mathcal{D}\rightarrow q_{D^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{D}
sending
\mathcal{F}\in(q^{*}_{D}\mathcal{D})_{T}\cong\mathcal{D}_{T\times_{S}D}

to
f^{*}\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{T\times_{S}D^{\prime}}
.
We also have the homotopy commutative diagram

\textstyle{q^{*}_{D}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{*}_{D}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{a_{f}^{*}}
\textstyle{q^{*}_{D}\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q_{f}^{*}}
\textstyle{q^{*}_{D^{\prime}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{*}_{D^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}
\textstyle{q^{*}_{D^{\prime}}\mathcal{D},}

where the functor
a_{f}^{*}
sends

(X\rightarrow T\times_{S}D)\in(q^{*}_{D}\mathrm{Ar})_{T}\cong(\mathrm{Ar}_{S})_{T\times_{S}D}
to the base change
X\times_{D}D^{\prime}\rightarrow T\times_{S}D^{\prime}
.
Moreover, the functor
Q_{f}^{*}
admits a right adjoint
Q_{f*}
relative to
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
in the sense of
[
HA
, 7.3.2.2]
.

2.

Assume
f\colon D^{\prime}\rightarrow D
is
smooth
of relative dimension
d
.
Then,
Q_{f}^{*}
admits a left adjoint
Q_{f!}\!\left<d\right>\colon q_{D^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{D}\rightarrow q_{D}^{*}\mathcal{D}
relative to
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}

and the homotopy commutative diagram

\textstyle{q_{D^{\prime}}^{*}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{D^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{a_{f!}}
\textstyle{q_{D^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q_{f!}}
\textstyle{q_{D}^{*}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{D}^{*}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}
\textstyle{q_{D}^{*}\mathcal{D},}

where the functor
a_{f!}
sends
X\rightarrow T\times_{S}D^{\prime}
to the composition

X\rightarrow T\times_{S}D^{\prime}\rightarrow T\times_{S}D
.

3.

We have the natural transform
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{*}
of functors
\mathrm{Ar}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
.
Let
\iota\colon\mathrm{Ar}_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Ar}_{S}
be the full subcategory spanned by proper morphisms
X\rightarrow T
.
Then
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\circ\iota\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{*}\circ\iota
is an equivalence.

2.13

Let
s,t\colon\mathrm{Ar}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
be the functor such that
s(X\rightarrow T)=X
and
t(X\rightarrow T)=T
.
We have the functor
F\colon\mathrm{Mdf}_{S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ar}
sending
(V\rightarrow T\times\Box)
to
V\rightarrow T
of Cartesian fibrations over
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
Each fiber of this functor commutes with pullbacks and is continuous.
Thus, by Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
A.8
the composition with
F
induces the functor

(\circ F)\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathrm{Sch}_{S}}(\mathrm{Ar})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathrm{Sch}_{S}}(\mathrm{Mdf}_{S})
, using the notations therein.
We put
\mathcal{V}:=s\circ F
and
\mathcal{T}:=t\circ F
the functors
\mathrm{Mdf}_{S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
If we are given a functor
r\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
and a functor
G\colon\mathrm{Mdf}_{S}\rightarrow r^{*}\mathrm{Ar}
,
we denote

.

2.16 Lemma
. —

1.

The morphisms
\mathcal{F}^{x}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{x}_{x}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T})
and

\bigoplus_{x\in|\Box|}\mathcal{F}^{x}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T})
are equivalences.

2.

For
x\in|\Box|
, fix a rational point
h_{x}\in(\Box\setminus\{x\})(k)
.
Then we have a map
\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathrm{Sch}}(\mathrm{Mdf})
depicted as

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}^{x}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\bigl({}^{\underline{z}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T},1)}\underline{\mathcal{H}}^{*}(\mathcal{T}\times\Box/\mathcal{T})^{\star}\bigr).}

This functor is defined canonically up to contractible space of choices.

Proof.

Let us show the first claim.
In order to show the morphism is an equivalence, it suffices to show that
r(V\rightarrow T\times\Box)

is an equivalence for any
(V\rightarrow T\times\Box)
in
\mathrm{Mdf}
, where
r
is one of the either morphisms in the statement.
Let
i\colon V_{\mathrm{fl}}\hookrightarrow V
be the closed immersion and
j\colon U:=V\setminus V_{\mathrm{fl}}\hookrightarrow V
be the complement.
For
\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{D}_{V}
, let
\mathcal{H}^{*}(V,\mathcal{G}):=\pi_{*}\mathcal{G}
, where
\pi\colon V\rightarrow T
is the structural morphism.
We have the localization sequence in
\mathcal{D}_{V}

j_{!}R_{U}\rightarrow R_{V}\rightarrow i_{*}R_{V_{\mathrm{fl}}},

which implies that
\mathcal{H}^{*}(V,j_{!}R_{U})\simeq\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{V})(V\rightarrow T\times\Box)
.
Now, by definition,
U=\coprod_{x\in|\Box|}U_{x}
.
This implies that
j_{!}R_{U}\simeq\bigoplus j_{x!}R_{U_{x}}
, where
j_{x}\colon U_{x}\hookrightarrow V
,
and
\mathcal{H}^{*}(V,j_{!}R_{U})\simeq\bigoplus\mathcal{H}^{*}(V,j_{x!}R_{U_{x}})
.
Again by the localization sequence, we have

\mathcal{H}^{*}(V,j_{x!}R_{U_{x}})\simeq\mathcal{F}^{x}(\mathcal{V})(V\rightarrow T\times\Box)
, and the second equivalence follows.
The first equivalence follows since both are equivalent to
\mathcal{H}^{*}(V,j_{x!}R_{U_{x}})\simeq\mathcal{H}^{*}(V_{x},j_{x!}R_{U_{x}})
.

Let us show the second claim.
For any
y\in\Box(k)
, (
2.14.i
) induces the homotopy commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}^{x}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\star}\circ\bigl({}^{\underline{z}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T},1)}\underline{\mathcal{H}}^{*}(\mathcal{T}\times\Box/\mathcal{T})^{\star}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}^{x}_{y}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{y}/\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\star}\circ\bigl({}^{\underline{z}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T},1)}\underline{\mathcal{H}}^{*}(\mathcal{T}\otimes k(y)/\mathcal{T})^{\star}\bigr).}

Since the cohomology theory is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopic and
k(y)=k
,
the right vertical morphism
\gamma
is an equivalence.
Now, take
y=h_{x}
.
Then the morphism
\mathcal{V}_{y}\rightarrow(\mathcal{V}^{x})_{y}
is an isomorphism, which implies that the edge
\star

of the diagram (
2.15.i
) is an equvalence.
This yields a homotopy
\mathcal{F}^{x}_{y}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T})\simeq 0
in essentially unique way.
By choosing a quasi-inverse to
\gamma
, which is determined essentially uniquely as well,
we get the desired diagram.
∎

2.19 Theorem
. —

Assume we are in the situation of
2.10
.
Furthermore, assume we are given a Cartesian fibration
a\colon\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
,
and a functor
F\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
which preserves coCartesian edges over
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
We define
H(F):=M_{F}(R)\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, where
R:=\mathcal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{T}/\mathcal{T})\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
.
Then, we may endow
H(F)
with a rewind structure in the sense of Definition
1.8
.

\partial_{x}M_{F}(R)\rightarrow M_{F}\bigl(\widetilde{\partial}_{x}(R)\bigr)

.

We have morphisms
