2.20 Definition
. —

Let
f\colon X\rightarrow S
be a separated morphism of finite type.
Consider the composition
\underline{\mathrm{H}}^{\mathrm{BM}}_{X/S}\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
,
where the 1st functor sends
T
to
X_{T}\rightarrow T
and the 2nd functor is the one in
C.3
.
This functor is equipped with a rewind structure by the theorem, and in fact a reversible coefficient.
Indeed, it is a cdh-sheaf by Lemma
C.7
, and
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant
since the functor is an
\infty
-enhancement of the Borel-Moore homology theory.

3
Microlocalization

In this section, we develop a general theory to “microlocalize” a map
\mathscr{S}\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{H}}^{\mathrm{BM}}_{X/S}
where
\mathscr{S}

is an abelian sheaf on
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
and
X\rightarrow S
is a morphism of schemes.
This section is inspired strongly by the work of Beilinson in
[
B1
]
.
We fix a base scheme
S
, which is noetherian separated and of finite Krull dimension.

3.1

For a vector bundle
E\rightarrow X
over a scheme
X
, we denote by
\mathcal{O}(E)
the abelian presheaf on
\mathrm{Sch}_{/X}

which associates
\mathrm{Hom}_{Y}(Y,E\times_{X}Y)
to
Y\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/X}
.
Thus, if we are given
f\in\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{O}(E))
, we have the associated morphism
Y\rightarrow E_{Y}:=E\times_{X}Y
.

Definition
. —

Let
X
be an
S
-scheme separated of finite type.
We say that
(E,\nu,\mathrm{d}_{\nu})
is a
base data of microlocalization over
X
if

1.

E
is a vector bundle (of rank
r
) over
X
;

2.

\nu
is an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module on
\mathrm{Sch}_{/X}
equipped with homomorphism
\mathrm{d}_{\nu}\colon\nu\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(E)

of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules.

For
A\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
and
\mathbf{f}\in\Gamma(A,\nu)
, we also denote by
\mathrm{d}_{\nu}(\mathbf{f})\colon A\rightarrow E

the morphism associated to
\mathrm{d}_{\nu}(\mathbf{f})\in\Gamma(A,\mathcal{O}(E))
.
We often denote
\mathrm{d}_{\nu}
simply by
\mathrm{d}
.
A morphism
(E,\nu,\mathrm{d}_{\nu})\rightarrow(E^{\prime},\nu^{\prime},\mathrm{d}_{\nu^{\prime}})
of base data consists of
morphisms
f\colon E\rightarrow E^{\prime}
and
g\colon\nu\rightarrow\nu^{\prime}
such that
\mathrm{d}_{\nu^{\prime}}\circ g=f\circ\mathrm{d}_{\nu}
.

Note that since
\mathrm{d}_{\nu}
is a homomorphism, for
A\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/X}
and
\mathbf{f},\mathbf{f}^{\prime}\in\Gamma(A,\nu)
,
the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}(\mathbf{f}+\mathbf{f}^{\prime})}
\textstyle{E}
\textstyle{A\times_{X}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{f}\times\mathrm{d}\mathbf{f}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{E\times_{X}E.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+}

Example
. —

For a smooth scheme
X
over
S
,
(T^{*}(X/S),\mathcal{O}_{X/S},\mathrm{d}_{X/S})
, where
\mathrm{d}_{X/S}\colon\mathcal{O}_{X/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(T^{*}(X/S))\cong\Omega^{1}_{X/S}

is the differential, is a base data of microlocalization over
X
relative to
S
.

3.2 Definition
. —

1.

Let
\mathrm{Sect}
be the category whose objects consist of triples
(U,A,\mathbf{f})
where
U\subset X
is an open subscheme,

A
is a finite dimensional affine space on
S
(
i.e.
there exists
n\geq 0
such that
A\cong\mathbb{A}^{n}_{S}
),
and a section
\mathbf{f}\in\Gamma(U\times_{S}A,\nu)
.
A morphism
(U,A,\mathbf{f})\rightarrow(U^{\prime},A^{\prime},\mathbf{f}^{\prime})
is an inclusion
U\subset U^{\prime}
and an affine morphism
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
such that
the restriction morphism
\Gamma(U^{\prime}\times_{S}A^{\prime},\nu)\rightarrow\Gamma(U\times_{S}A,\nu)
sends
\mathbf{f}^{\prime}
to
\mathbf{f}
.

2.

For a scheme
T
, let
\mathrm{Sm}_{T}
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Sch}_{/T}
spanned by smooth morphisms
Y\rightarrow T
.
Let
C\subset E
be a closed subscheme.
We have the functor
D_{C}\colon\mathrm{Sect}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Sm}_{X})
,
often denoted simply by
D
, sending
(U,A,\mathbf{f})
to the Cartesian diagram in
\mathrm{Sm}_{X}

\textstyle{U_{\mathbf{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{E_{U}\setminus C_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U\times_{S}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{f}}
\textstyle{E_{U},}

where
E_{U}:=E\times_{S}U
,
C_{U}:=C\times_{S}U
.

3.3

Before moving further, let us make a categorical digression.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category, and let
\delta\colon\mathbf{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
be the functor sending
[n]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
to
\Delta^{n}
.
We define
\mathrm{Sd}(\mathcal{C})
to be
\mathbf{\Delta}\times_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})_{/\mathcal{C}}
.
If
\mathcal{C}
is a
1
-category,
\mathrm{Sd}(\mathcal{C})
is (equivalent to) a
1
-category as well.
Assume we are given a diagram
p\colon K^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
of
\infty
-categories for some simplicial set
K
.
Then let
{}^{\backprime}\mathrm{Sd}(p):=(K^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathbf{\Delta})\times_{p\times\delta,\mathrm{Fun}(\partial\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})}\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})
.
The map
{}^{\backprime}\mathrm{Sd}(p)\rightarrow K^{\mathrm{op}}
is a coCartesian fibration by
[
HTT
, 2.4.7.10, 2.4.7.5]

(see also footnote
(3)
in
1.10
).
Its dual Cartesian fibration is denoted by
\mathrm{Sd}(p)\rightarrow K
.
If
p
is the straightening of a Cartesian fibration
Y\rightarrow K
, we denote
\mathrm{Sd}(p)
by
\mathrm{Sd}(Y/K)
.
Note that for a vertex
s\in K
, we have
\mathrm{Sd}(Y/K)_{s}\simeq\mathrm{Sd}(Y_{s})
.

Remark
. —

Unwinding the definition, the fiber over
s\in K
is the
\infty
-category whose objects are functors
\Delta^{m}\rightarrow Y_{s}
for some
m
,
and edges are
homotopy commutative
diagrams of the form

\textstyle{\Delta^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y_{s}}
\textstyle{\Delta^{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}

in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
.
If this were a commutative diagram of simplicial sets,
\mathrm{Sd}(Y_{s})
would be the category of simplices of
Y_{s}
.
This observation implies that if
Y_{s}
is a
1
-category such that any isomorphism is the identity,
then
\mathrm{Sd}(Y_{s})
is the category of simplicies.
In particular, the simplicial set
\mathrm{Sd}(Y_{s})
is homotopic to
Y_{s}
by
[
HTT
, 4.2.3.14, 4.1.1.3]
.
In fact, this homotopy equivalence holds for any
\infty
-category
Y_{s}
, but we do not need this in this paper.

3.4 Definition
. —

1.

We define a simplicial set
\mathrm{Frm}
as follows.
The set of
0
-vertices
\mathrm{Frm}_{0}
is equal to
(U,\mathbb{A}^{r}_{S},\mathbf{f})\in\mathrm{Sect}

such that the induced morphism
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{f}\colon U\times_{S}\mathbb{A}^{r}_{S}\rightarrow E_{U}:=E\times_{X}U

is an
isomorphism of vector bundles
over
U
.
In other words, we can write
\mathbf{f}=\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}f_{i}x_{i}
,
where
f_{i}\in\Gamma(U,\nu)
and
x_{i}
is the coordinate of
\mathbb{A}^{r}
, such that
\{\mathrm{d}_{\nu}(f_{i})\}
is a basis of
E\times_{X}U
.
An element of
\mathrm{Frm}_{0}
is called a
frame over
U
.

Let
X_{\mathrm{zar}}
be the category of (Zariski) open sets of
X
.
We have the map
\mathrm{Frm}_{0}\rightarrow\mathrm{N}(X_{\mathrm{zar}})_{0}
sending
(U,\mathbb{A}^{r},\mathbf{f})
to
U
.
We define a simplicial set
\mathrm{Frm}
by
\mathrm{N}(X_{\mathrm{zar}})\times_{\mathrm{cosk}_{0}\mathrm{N}(X_{\mathrm{zar}})_{0}}\mathrm{cosk}_{0}\mathrm{Frm}_{0}
.
In fact,
\mathrm{Frm}
is (the nerve of) the category whose set of objects are equal to
\mathrm{Frm}_{0}
and the set of morphisms

\mathrm{Hom}\bigl((U,\mathbb{A}^{r}_{S},\mathbf{f}),(U^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{r}_{S},\mathbf{f}^{\prime})\bigr)
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Hom}(U,U^{\prime})
.
By construction, we have the map
\mathrm{Frm}\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{zar}}
, which is in fact a right fibration.

2.

The base data is said to
have enough frames
if the sieve

\{U\in X_{\mathrm{zar}}\mid\mathrm{Frm}\times_{X_{\mathrm{zar}}}\{U\}\neq\emptyset\}
is a Zariski covering sieve of
X
.
The full subcategory of
X_{\mathrm{zar}}
spanned by the above set is denoted by
X_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{fr}}
.

Now, let us construct a functor
\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sect}
over
X_{\mathrm{zar}}
(of
1
-categories).
First, fix
U\in X_{\mathrm{zar}}
and let us construct a functor
\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}_{U})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sect}_{U}
.
Giving a vertex of
\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}_{U})
is equivalent to giving frames
\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{n}\,(\in\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{r}_{U},\nu))
over
U
for some
n\geq 0
.
We denote this vertex by
(U,\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{n})
.
Let
p^{n}_{i}\colon\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{r}
be the projection sending
(t_{j})_{1\leq j\leq r(n+1)}
to
(t_{j})_{ri<j\leq r(i+1)}
.
We put
\mathbf{g}:=\sum(p_{i}^{n})^{*}\mathbf{f}_{i}\in\Gamma(U\times\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)},\nu)
.
Using these, we have an object
(U,\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)},\mathbf{g})
of
\mathrm{Sect}
.
This assignment
(U,\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)},\mathbf{g})
to
(U,\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{n})
defines a map

F\colon\mathrm{Ob}(\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}_{U}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Ob}(\mathrm{Sect}_{U})
.
Let us construct a map between Hom-sets to promote
F
to a functor between categories.
Giving a morphism in
\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}_{U})
is the same as giving a map
\phi\colon[n]\rightarrow[m]
and

(U,\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{m})\in\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}_{U})_{m}
.
Indeed, this data gives us a morphism

\alpha\colon(U,\mathbf{f}_{\phi(0)},\mathbf{f}_{\phi(1)},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{\phi(n)})\rightarrow(U,\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{m})
in

\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}_{U})
.
Let us write a point of
\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)}
as
(\mathbf{s}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{s}_{n})
, where
\mathbf{s}_{i}\in\mathbb{A}^{r}
.
The map
\phi
induces the morphism
z_{\phi}\colon\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{r(m+1)}

sending
(\mathbf{s}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{s}_{n})
to
(\mathbf{t}_{j})
where
\mathbf{t}_{j}=\sum_{i\in\phi^{-1}(j)}\mathbf{s}_{i}

if
j\in\mathrm{Im}(\phi)
and
\mathbf{t}_{j}=0
if
j\neq\mathrm{Im}(\phi)
.
We wish to define
F(\alpha)
as

\displaystyle F(U,\mathbf{f}_{\phi(0)},\mathbf{f}_{\phi(1)},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{\phi(n)})

\displaystyle=(U,\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)},\sum(p^{n}_{i})^{*}\mathbf{f}_{\phi(i)})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id},z_{\phi})}(U,\mathbb{A}^{r(m+1)},\sum(p_{j}^{m})^{*}\mathbf{f}_{j})=F(U,\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{m}).

In order that the morphism
(\mathrm{id},z_{\phi})
indeed defines a morphism in
\mathrm{Sect}
,
we must check that
z_{\phi}^{*}(\sum(p_{i}^{m})^{*}\mathbf{f}_{i})=\sum(p_{i}^{n})^{*}\mathbf{f}_{\phi(i)}
.
Since
(U,\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{m})
belongs to
\mathrm{Frm}
,
recall that
\mathbf{f}_{i}
is linear with respect the coordinates of
\mathbb{A}^{r}
.
This implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq i\leq n}(p_{i}^{n})^{*}\mathbf{f}_{\phi(i)}=\sum_{j\in\mathrm{Im}(\phi)}\sum_{i\in\phi^{-1}(j)}(p_{i}^{n})^{*}\mathbf{f}_{j}=\sum_{j\in\mathrm{Im}(\phi)}(p^{m}_{j}\circ z_{\phi})^{*}\mathbf{f}_{j}=\sum_{0\leq j\leq m}(p^{m}_{j}\circ z_{\phi})^{*}\mathbf{f}_{j}.

This defines a functor
F_{U}\colon\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}_{U})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sect}_{U}
.
If we are given a morphism
j\colon U^{\prime}\rightarrow U
, we have
j^{*}\circ F_{U}=F_{U^{\prime}}\circ j^{*}
, not only an equivalence,
and defines a functor
\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sect}
by unstraightening,
or Grothendieck construction since we are in the situation of
1
-categories.

Now, consider the composition

\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sect}\xrightarrow{D}\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Sm}_{X})
.
We may write this diagram
\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}}),\mathrm{Sm}_{X})
as:

\textstyle{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{(E\setminus C)_{\mathcal{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{f}}
\textstyle{E_{\mathcal{U}},}

where the vertices correspond to those of the diagram of Definition
3.2
.
2
.
For example, the value of
\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{f}}
at
(U,\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{n})\in\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}})
is
U_{\mathbf{g}}
,
where
\mathbf{g}=(\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{n})
in
\Gamma(U\times\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)},\nu)
.
The following easy fact is a key to our construction:

Lemma
. —

The evaluation of the map
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{f}\colon\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{U}}\rightarrow E_{\mathcal{U}}

at any object of
\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}})
is an affine bundle.

Proof.

Let
(U,\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{f}_{n})\in\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}})
,
and put
\mathbf{g}:=\sum p_{i}^{*}\mathbf{f}_{n}
in
\Gamma(U\times\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)},\nu)
.
We must show that the morphism
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{g}\colon U\times\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)}\rightarrow E_{U}
is an affine bundle.
Indeed,
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{g}
is equal to the composition

\mathbb{A}^{r(n+1)}\cong(\mathbb{A}^{r})^{\times(n+1)}\xrightarrow[\sim]{(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{f}_{0},\dots,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{f}_{n})}E^{\times(n+1)}\xrightarrow{\sum}E.

The last morphism is an
\mathbb{A}^{rn}
-bundle, thus the claim follows.
∎

3.5

Now, assume we are in the situation of
2.10
.
Let
f\colon X\rightarrow S
be the structural morphism.
We put
\omega_{X/S}:=f^{!}R_{S}
in
\mathcal{D}_{X}
.
Recall from
C.5
that we have the functor
\mathrm{H}^{*}(-,\omega_{X/S})\colon\mathrm{Sm}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}

informally sending
g\colon Y\rightarrow X
to
\mathrm{H}^{*}(Y,\omega_{X/S})\simeq\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{D}_{Y}}(R_{Y},g^{*}\omega_{X/S})
.
We finally consider the following composition

\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Sm}_{X})^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{H}^{*}(,\omega_{X/S})}\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Mod}_{R}).

This functor can be depicted as

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{f}}/X,\omega_{X/S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\star

○
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{*}((E\setminus C)_{\mathcal{U}}/X,\omega_{X/S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{U}}/X,\omega_{X/S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{*}(E_{\mathcal{U}}/X,\omega_{X/S}).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{f})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}

Here the equivalences of the horizontal edges follow by Lemma
3.4
.
We often omit
\omega_{X/S}
from
\mathrm{H}^{*}(-,\omega_{X/S})
in the following.

Lemma
. —

Consider the functor

\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{f}}/X)\colon\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathrm{op}}\cong\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, and the left Kan extension functor

p_{!}\colon\mathrm{Fun}\bigl(\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\bigr)\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}\bigl((X_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\bigr).

with respect to the functor
p\colon\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{zar}})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow(X^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{zar}})^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Then we have a canonical equivalence

p_{!}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{f}}/X)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{*}((E\setminus C)_{\mathcal{U}}/X)|_{(X_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathrm{op}}}

up to contractible space of choices.
Furthermore, if the base data have enough frames, then we have

\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{(X_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathrm{op}}}p_{!}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{f}}/X)\simeq\mathrm{H}^{*}((E\setminus C)_{\mathcal{U}}/X)
.

Proof.

We have the maps

p_{!}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{f}}/X)\xleftarrow{\sim}p_{!}\mathrm{H}^{*}((E\setminus C)_{\mathcal{U}}/X)|_{(X_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{*}((E\setminus C)_{\mathcal{U}}/X)|_{(X_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathrm{op}}}.

It suffices to show that the second map is an equivalence.
Since
p
is a coCartesian fibration,
p_{!}
can be computed fiberwise.
Thus, it suffices to show that the fiber of
p
over any object of
X_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{fr}}

is weakly contractible by
[
HTT
, 4.4.4.10]
.
For a fixed
U\in X^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{zar}}
, the category
\mathrm{Frm}\times_{X_{\mathrm{zar}}}\{U\}
is Kan contractible by definition.
Since any isomorphism in
\mathrm{Frm}\times_{X_{\mathrm{zar}}}\{U\}
is the identity,
the category
\mathrm{Sd}(\mathrm{Frm}/X_{\mathrm{zar}})_{U}
is weakly homotopic to
\mathrm{Frm}\times_{X_{\mathrm{zar}}}\{U\}
by Remark
Remark
.
The final claim follows by Zariski descent of
\mathrm{H}^{*}(,\omega_{X/S})
.
∎

3.6

With the preparation so far, let us return to the theory of microlocalizations.
Let
(X,E,\nu,\mathrm{d}_{\nu})
be a base data of microlocalization,
and assume we are given a morphism
\mathscr{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
of presheaves on
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
For
\mathcal{F}\in\Gamma(S,\mathscr{S})
, we wish to talk about the “support of
\mathcal{F}
on
X
”.
In order to do this, we need additional structure on
\mathscr{S}
.

Let
(X/S)_{\mathrm{zar}}
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Sch}_{/k})_{/(X\rightarrow S)}

spanned by morphisms
U\rightarrow T
such that the induced morphism
U\rightarrow X\times_{S}T
is an open immersion.
We have the functor
(X/S)_{\mathrm{zar}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
sending
U\rightarrow T
to
T
.
This is a Cartesian fibration which admits finite limits in each fiber and for each edge of
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}

the associated functor between the fibers commutes with finite limits.

Definition
. —

Let
(X,E,\nu,\mathrm{d}_{\nu})
be a base data of microlocalization.

1.

Let
\mathscr{S}
be an abelian presheaf on
(X/S)_{\mathrm{zar}}
.
A

(
with respect to the base data
)
is a homomorphism
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\colon\mathscr{S}\otimes\nu\rightarrow\partial\mathscr{S}
,
where
\nu
is considered as a presheaf of sets to take
\otimes
as usual,
such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathscr{S}\otimes\nu\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma}
\textstyle{\partial\mathscr{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{0}\circ I}
\textstyle{\partial_{0}\mathscr{S}.}

Here,
\Sigma
is the morphism induced by the unique map
\nu\rightarrow\{*\}
.
We denote by
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{x}
the composition
\mathscr{S}\otimes\nu\xrightarrow{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\partial\mathscr{S}\xrightarrow{r_{x}}\partial_{x}\mathscr{S}
.

2.

Let
(\mathscr{S},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be a presheaf with support structure with respect to the base data.
Let

be a conic in

.
An element

if for any

such that

is

and any

0
.
We denote by
\Gamma(X,\mathscr{S})_{C}
, or more precisely
\Gamma(X,\mathscr{S})_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime},C}
,
the subgroup of
\Gamma(X,\mathscr{S})
consisting of elements which are microsupported on
C
.

Recall from
C.4
that we have the functor
{}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\colon\widehat{(X/S)}\vphantom{X}_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}

which preserves coCartesian edges over
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Consider the functor
H({}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*})\colon(X/S)^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{zar}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}

by

, which we view as a

-valued presheaf on
(X/S)_{\mathrm{zar}}
.
