Comparison with Saito’s characteristic cycles

In this final section, we compare the microlocalized characteristic cycle we constructed with that of T. Saito.
We keep using the ring
\Lambda
from the previous section.

6.1

Let us recall the definition of singular support:

Definition

(Beilinson
[
B2
]
)
. —

Let
X
be a smooth scheme over a field
k
, and
\mathcal{F}
be a constructible
\Lambda
-module on
X
.

1.

Let
C\subset T^{*}X
be a closed conic subset.
A pair of morphisms
(X\xleftarrow{f}U\xrightarrow{g}Y)
is said to be
C
-transversal at
u\in U

if
\mathrm{Ker}(\mathrm{d}f)\cap C=0
in
T^{*}X\times_{X}\{u\}
and
(\mathrm{d}g)^{-1}(f^{\circ}C)=0
in
T^{*}Y\times_{Y}\{u\}
,
where
f^{\circ}C:=\mathrm{Im}(C\times_{X}U\hookrightarrow T^{*}X\times_{X}U\xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}f}T^{*}U)
.

2.

We define the
singular support of
\mathcal{F}
denoted by
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
to be the smallest conic in
T^{*}X
having the following property:
For any pair
(X\xleftarrow{f}U\xrightarrow{g}Y)
and
u\in U
which is
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
-transversal at
u\in U
,

g
is universally locally acyclic at
u
with respect to
f^{*}\mathcal{F}
.

The existence of singular support is not too hard to check, but surprisingly, the main result of Beilinson states that

\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
is equidimensional and
\dim(\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F}))=\dim(X)
.

6.2

Now, let us recall the definition of characteristic cycle:

Definition

(Saito
[
S1
, Def 5.10]
)
. —

Let
X
be a smooth scheme of finite type over a
perfect field

k
equidimensional of dimension
d
,
and
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(X,\Lambda)
.

1.

Let
C
be a conic closed subset of
T^{*}X
.
A point
x\in X
is said to be an
isolated
C
-characteristic point
of a function
f\colon X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}

if
x
is an isolated point in
\iota_{X}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}f)^{-1}(C)
. Here
\iota_{X}\colon X\hookrightarrow T^{*}\mathbb{A}^{1}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X

is the section defined by
\mathrm{d}s
using the coordinate
s
of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

2.

Write
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})=\bigcup_{i\in I}W_{i}
by irreducible components where
W_{i}
is integral.
There exists a unique number in
n_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}[1/p]
for each
i\in I
such that,
for any finite extension
l/k
of fields,
for any local function
f\colon U\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
where
U\subset X\otimes_{k}l
is an open subscheme,
and for any isolated
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
-characteristic point
x\in U
of
f
,
we have the equality

(6.2.i)

-\mathrm{totdim}\bigl(\Phi_{f}(\mathcal{F}|_{U})_{\overline{x}}\bigr)=\bigl(\mathrm{d}f,\sum n_{i}[W_{i}]\bigr)_{T^{*}U,x},

where
\mathrm{totdim}
denotes the total dimension (
i.e.

\mathrm{totdim}(V)=\dim(V)+\mathrm{Sw}(V)
),
and the right hand side denotes the intersection number in
T^{*}U
at
x
.
We define
\mathrm{CC}(\mathcal{F}):=\sum n_{i}[W_{i}]
in
\mathrm{Z}_{d}(T^{*}X)[1/p]
.

Remark
. —

In fact, Saito uses étale local functions with target scheme arbitrary smooth curve,
contrary to using only Zariski local functions on
X\otimes_{k}l
with target scheme
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, to define characteristic cycles.
Thus,
a priori
, it is not clear if the numbers
n_{i}
are uniquely determined in our definition.
However, they are in fact unique.
To see this, note that it suffices show the existence in the case where
k
is algebraically closed.
In this situation, Saito checks conditions of
[
S1
, Prop 5.8.1]
for the
\mathbb{Z}[1/p]
-valued function on
isolated
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
-characteristic points defined by the left hand side of (
6.2.i
) above,
and
[
S1
, Prop 5.8.1]
uses only Zariski-local functions as in our definition.
Moreover, in view of
[
S1
, Prop 5.8.2]
, we may assume that the target of the function is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

6.3

First, let us reformulate results of Laumon on local Fourier transform, which is a crucial observation for us:

Theorem

(Laumon)
. —

Let
k
be a perfect field, let
T:=(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k})_{(0)}

(
resp.
T^{\prime}:=(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k})_{(0)}
)

be the henselization with coordinate
t

(
resp.
t^{\prime}
)
,
and let
T\xleftarrow{\mathrm{pr_{1}}}T\times_{k}T^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}T^{\prime}
be the projections.
For
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(T)
, we have

-\mathrm{totdim}(\Phi_{\mathrm{id}}(\mathcal{F}))=\dim\bigl(\Psi_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{!}(t/t^{\prime}))_{(\overline{0},\overline{0}^{\prime})}\bigr).

Here,
\mathcal{L}_{!}(t/t^{\prime})
is the zero-extension of
\mathcal{L}(t/t^{\prime})
on
T\times T^{\prime}\setminus T\times\{0^{\prime}\}
.

Proof.

Since the invariants appearing in the equality are stable under taking the reduction, we may assume that
\Lambda
is a field.
Thus, we may assume that
\mathcal{F}
is a constructible sheaf.
Let
i\colon\{0\}\hookrightarrow T
be the closed point and
j\colon\eta\hookrightarrow T
be the generic point.
By Brauer’s theory, we may take a lift
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\eta}\in D^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\eta,E)
,
where
E
is a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
whose residue field is
\Lambda
,
such that the reduction of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\eta}
is equal to
\mathcal{F}_{\eta}:=j^{*}\mathcal{F}

in the Grothendieck group
\mathrm{K}_{0}D^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\eta,\Lambda)
.

Now, we have

\mathrm{totdim}(\Phi_{\mathrm{id}}(\mathcal{F}))=\mathrm{totdim}(\mathcal{F}_{\eta})-\dim(\mathcal{F}_{\overline{0}})=\mathrm{totdim}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\eta})-\dim(\mathcal{F}_{\overline{0}})
.
On the other hand, we have
\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{!}(t/t^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{F}_{0}\otimes j^{\prime}_{!}j^{\prime*}\Lambda_{0\times T^{\prime}}
.
This implies that
\Psi_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{!}(t/t^{\prime}))_{(\overline{0},\overline{0}^{\prime})}\cong\mathcal{F}_{\overline{0}}
.
Since
\mathcal{L}_{!}(t/t^{\prime})
is
0
on
\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{-1}(0^{\prime})
, we have
\Psi_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\dots)\cong\Phi_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\dots)
.
Recall the definition of local Fourier transform from
[
La
, 2.4.2.3]
.
Using this, we can compute as

\displaystyle\dim

\displaystyle\bigl(\Psi_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}(t/t^{\prime}))_{(\overline{0},\overline{0}^{\prime})}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\dim\bigl(\Psi_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}j_{!}j^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}(t/t^{\prime}))_{(\overline{0},\overline{0}^{\prime})}\bigr)+\dim\bigl(\Psi_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}(t/t^{\prime}))_{(\overline{0},\overline{0}^{\prime})}\bigr)

\displaystyle=-\dim_{E}\mathscr{F}^{(0,\infty^{\prime})}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\eta})+\dim_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{F}_{\overline{0}}),

where we used the vanishing result
[
La
, 2.4.2.2]
at the last equality.
Invoking
[
La
, 2.4.3 (i)-(b)]
, the theorem follows.
∎

6.4

We need the following basic result:

Lemma
. —

Let
g\colon X\rightarrow Y
be a morphism of schemes
(
not necessary of finite type
)
.
Let
\mathcal{L}
be a locally constant constructible flat
\Lambda
-module on
Y
.
Then for any
\mathcal{F}\in D^{+}(X,\Lambda)
on
X
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{R}g_{*}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{R}g_{*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes g^{*}\mathcal{L}).

Proof.

The construction of the homomorphism is standard using adjoints.
Since, now, the claim is local, we may assume that
Y
is the spectrum
of a strictly henselian ring. In this situation, we may assume that

\mathcal{L}
is the constant sheaf
\Lambda^{\oplus n}
, in which case, the
claim is obvious.
∎

6.5 Proposition
. —

Let
X
,
S
be schemes of finite type over a perfect field
k
such that
S
is smooth and integral.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be an object of
D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(X)
,
and
f\colon X\times S\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}
be an
S
-morphism, in other words, a “function parameterized by
S
”.
Let
x\in X
,
s\in S
be points, and we denote by
f_{s}:=f|_{X\times\{s\}}
.
We put
\Psi_{(x,s)}:=\Psi_{(s,\infty)\leftarrow\eta}\bigl(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft)\bigr)_{\overline{x}}
,
where the nearby cycle is taken with respect to the projection

X\times S\times\Box\rightarrow S\times\Box
,
\eta
is the generic point of
S\times\Box
, and
\mathcal{L}_{!}
is the zero extension of
\mathcal{L}
.

1.

Assume
f_{s}
is
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
-transversal at
x

(
namely,
f_{s}
is smooth at
x
and
df_{s}(x)\cap\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})=\{0\}
in
T^{*}_{x}X
)
.
Then
\Psi_{(x,s)}=0
.

2.

Assume
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
and
x
is an isolated characteristic point of
f
.
Then
\dim(\Psi_{x})=-\mathrm{totdim}(\Phi_{f}(\mathcal{F}))
.

Proof.

Let us initialize the notation for a moment.
Let
S
be a spectrum of a noetherian henselian local scheme, and
Y\rightarrow S
be a morphism of finite type.
Let
g\colon S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
be a morphism of noetherian henselian local schemes,
and take geometric points
y\rightarrow Y
,
s^{\prime}\rightarrow S^{\prime}
such that
g(s^{\prime})=f(y)
and
s^{\prime}
lies over the closed point of
S^{\prime}
.
Consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{(Y_{S^{\prime}})_{(y,s^{\prime})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{k}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{Y_{(y)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{S^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{S.}

Let
\Psi_{f^{\prime}}
be the nearby cycle functor
D^{+}(Z)\rightarrow D^{+}(Z\overleftarrow{\times}_{S^{\prime}}S^{\prime})
.
For
\mathcal{G}\in D^{+}(Z)
, we have
\mathrm{R}h_{*}(k^{*}\mathcal{G})\cong\Psi_{f^{\prime}}\mathcal{G}|_{(y,s^{\prime})\overleftarrow{\times}_{S^{\prime}}S^{\prime}}

by
[
I
, the next equation to (1.12.10)]
.
Now, let
\mathcal{F}
be an object of
D^{+}(Y_{(y)})
which is
f
-good.
The goodness implies that
\Psi_{f^{\prime}}g^{\prime*}\mathcal{F}\cong(g^{\prime}\overleftarrow{\times}_{g}g)^{*}\Psi_{f}\mathcal{F}
.
Thus, using the above formula of Illusie twice, we have
\mathrm{R}h_{*}k^{*}g^{\prime*}\mathcal{F}\cong g^{*}\mathrm{R}f_{*}\mathcal{F}
.

Now, let the notation be as in the statement of the proposition.
We denote the strict henselian scheme
(S\times\Box)_{(\overline{s},\overline{\infty})}
by
T
,
and abusing notation, let
\eta
be its generic point.
By definition of the nearby cycle functor,
\Psi_{(x,s)}
is isomorphic to

\mathrm{R}\Gamma\bigl((X\times S\times\Box)_{(\overline{x},\overline{s},\overline{\infty})}\times_{T}\overline{\eta},\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft)\bigl).

To compute this, we consider the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{(X\times S\times\Box)_{(\overline{x},\overline{s},\overline{\infty})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}=(f\times\mathrm{id})_{(\overline{x},\overline{s},\overline{\infty})}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr_{2}}}
\textstyle{(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{y}\times T)_{(f(\overline{x}),\overline{s},\overline{\infty})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{T=(S\times\Box)_{(\overline{s},\overline{\infty})}}

where
\pi
,
\mathrm{pr}_{2}
denote the projections.
We denote by
f^{\prime}_{\eta}
and
\pi_{\eta}
the fiber of
f^{\prime}
and
\pi
over
\eta
.
By smooth base change, we have

\mathrm{R}\Gamma\bigl((X\times S\times\Box)_{(\overline{x},\overline{s},\overline{\infty})}\times_{T}\overline{\eta},\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft)\bigl)\cong\bigl(\mathrm{R}\pi_{\eta*}\mathrm{R}f^{\prime}_{\eta*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}(ft))\bigr)_{\overline{\eta}}.

On the other hand, by Lemma
6.4
and the definition of
\mathcal{L}(ft)
, we have

\mathrm{R}f^{\prime}_{\eta*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}(ft))\cong\mathrm{R}f^{\prime}_{\eta*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes f^{\prime*}_{\eta}\mathcal{L}(yt))\cong\mathrm{R}f^{\prime}_{\eta*}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{L}(yt).

Now let
f_{(x,s)}\colon(X\times S)_{(\overline{x},\overline{s})}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S})_{(f(\overline{x}),\overline{s})}
be the canonical morphism.
We
assume that
\mathcal{F}
is
f_{(x,s)}
-good
.
Then the observation at the beginning implies that
\mathrm{R}f^{\prime}_{*}(\mathcal{F})\cong h^{*}\mathrm{R}f_{(x,s)*}(\mathcal{F})
,
where
h\colon(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times T)_{(f(\overline{x}),\overline{s},\overline{\infty})}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S})_{(f(\overline{x}),\overline{s})}
.
Summing up, under the goodness assumption, we have

(
\star
)

\Psi_{(x,s)}\cong\bigl(\mathrm{R}\pi_{\eta*}(h^{*}\mathrm{R}f_{(x,s)*}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{L}(yt))\bigr)_{\overline{\eta}}\cong\Psi_{(s,\infty)\leftarrow\eta}(h^{*}\mathrm{R}f_{(x,s)*}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{L}(yt)).

Let us show the second statement first.
In this case, since
(\mathbb{A}^{1})_{(f(x),s)}
is a henselian
trait
,
\mathcal{F}
is already good and we have (

⋆

‣
6.4
).
Invoking the result of Laumon, Theorem
6.3
, we get the result.

Let us show the first statement.
Under the assumption, we claim that
f_{(x,s)}
is universally locally acyclic with respect to
\mathcal{F}
.
Admitting this for a moment, this implies that
\mathcal{F}
is
f_{(x,s)}
-good, and we have the isomorphism (

⋆

‣
6.4
).
Furthermore,
\mathrm{R}f_{(x,s)*}(\mathcal{F})
is constant on
(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S})_{(f(x),s)}
, with value
\mathcal{F}_{\overline{x}}
,
by [SGA
4\frac{1}{2}
, Th. Finitude, A.2.6 and A.2.4].
By the theorem of Katz-Laumon (cf.
[
La
, 1.3.1.2]
), we know that

\Psi_{(s,\infty)\leftarrow\eta}(\mathcal{L}(yt))_{f(\overline{x})}=0
, and the result follows.
To conclude the proof, let us show the local acyclicity of
f_{(x,s)}
.

In fact, the claim follows easily from the definition of singular support as follows:
Consider the test pair
(X\xleftarrow{p_{1}}X\times S\xrightarrow{f}\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S})
.
By definition of singular support, it suffices to show that the test pair is
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
-transversal at

(x,s)\in X\times S
.
Since
p_{1}
is smooth,
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
is
p_{1}
-transversal,
and
p_{1}^{\circ}\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})=\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})\times S\cong\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})\times T^{*}_{S}S\subset T^{*}X\times T^{*}S
.
Let
(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})
and
(s_{1},\dots,s_{n})
be local coordinates of
X
and
S
at
x
and
s
.
Let
y
be the coordinate of
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}
.
Then
\mathrm{d}f(\mathrm{d}y)=\omega
such that
\omega(x,s)=\mathrm{d}f_{s}(x)+g_{1}\mathrm{d}s_{1}+\dots+g_{n}\mathrm{d}s_{n}
,
and
\mathrm{d}f(\mathrm{d}s_{i})=\mathrm{d}s_{i}
.
This presentation implies that

(\mathrm{d}f)^{-1}(\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})\times T^{*}_{S}S)\times_{S}\{s\}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathrm{d}f_{s})^{-1}(\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F}))\times\{s\},

where the morphism is induced by
T^{*}\mathbb{A}^{1}\times T^{*}S\rightarrow T^{*}\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S
.
Thus, the test pair is
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
-transversal at
(x,s)
if and only if
f_{s}
is
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
-transversal at
x
.
∎

6.6 Corollary
. —

Assume we are in the situation of
5.14
.
Assume further that the trace map of the motivic cohomology theory is chosen so that the induced map

z^{\mathrm{SV}}(\mathrm{Spec}(k)/\mathrm{Spec}(k),0)\rightarrow\pi_{0}\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathrm{Spec}(k)/\mathrm{Spec}(k))\cong\mathrm{CH}_{0}(\mathrm{Spec}(k))[1/p]

sends
[\mathrm{Spec}(k)]
to
[\mathrm{Spec}(k)]
, which exists by
[
A2
, 2.5, 5.4]
.
Let
X
be a smooth scheme over a perfect field
k
, and let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(X,\Lambda)
.

1.

The class
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{K}_{0}\mathrm{Cons}(X)
is microsupported on
\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})

in the sense of Definition
3.6
.

2.

We have
\mathrm{CC}^{\mu}_{X/\mathrm{Spec}(k)}([\mathcal{F}])=\mathrm{CC}(\mathcal{F})
.

Proof.

The first claim follows immediately from Proposition
6.5
.
1
.
For the second claim, we only need to check the Milnor formula (
6.2.i
) for
\mathrm{CC}^{\mu}_{X/\mathrm{Spec}(k)}([\mathcal{F}])
.
Let
f\colon U\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
be a morphism such that
U\subset X\otimes_{k}l
for some finite extension
l
of
k
as in Definition
6.2
.
Since
\mathrm{CC}^{\mu}_{X/\mathrm{Spec}(k)}
is stable under base change by étale extension of
k
, we may assume that
l=k
.
We wish to show that the left side of (
6.2.i
) is equal to
\bigl(\mathrm{d}f,\mathrm{CC}^{\mu}_{X/\mathrm{Spec}(k)}([\mathcal{F}])\bigr)
.
Let
Z:=\iota_{U}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}f)^{-1}(C)
, using the notation of Definition
6.2
, where
C=\mathrm{SS}(\mathcal{F})
.
By definition,
Z
is of dimension
0
.
This implies that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(-,f)
induces the homomorphism
\mathscr{S}(U)_{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}_{0}(U)
by Example
4.3
.
2
.
Put
V:=U\setminus Z
.
Consider the following homotopy commutative diagram:

\textstyle{z(Z,0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(Z,0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathscr{S}(U)_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(-,f)}
\textstyle{\mathscr{S}_{0}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{U}}
\textstyle{z(U,0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(U,0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{res}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{S}(V)_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(-,f)}
\textstyle{\mathscr{S}_{0}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{V}}
\textstyle{z(V,0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(V,0).}

Let
\phi
be the composition of the 2nd row.
Since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}([\mathcal{F}],f)|_{V}=0
, this yields a homotopy between
\mathrm{res}\circ\phi([\mathcal{F}])
and
0
.
Since the last column is a cofiber sequence, this homotopy determines an element in

\pi_{0}\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(U\setminus V,0)\cong\mathrm{CH}_{0}(U\setminus V)[1/p]\cong\bigoplus_{x\in|U\setminus V|}\mathbb{Z}[1/p]\cdot[x]
.
By construction, this element coincides with
\bigl(\mathrm{d}f,\mathrm{CC}^{\mu}_{X/\mathrm{Spec}(k)}([\mathcal{F}])\bigr)
.
Now, by definition,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}([\mathcal{F}],f)=\left[\Psi_{\infty\leftarrow\eta}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}(ft))\right]
,
where
\eta
is the generic point of
\Box
.
By Proposition
6.5
.
2
,
d_{U}\circ\mathcal{E}^{\prime}([\mathcal{F}],f)
coincides with the cycle

-\sum_{x}\mathrm{totdim}\bigl(\Phi_{f}(\mathcal{F}|_{U})_{\overline{x}}\bigr)\cdot[x]
in
z(U\setminus V)
.
Thus, the Milnor formula holds for
\mathrm{CC}^{\mu}_{X/\mathrm{Spec}(k)}([\mathcal{F}])
, which terminates the proof.
∎

Remark
. —

In view of Theorem
5.13
, we have the pushforward formula for
\mathrm{CC}^{\mu}_{X/\mathrm{Spec}(k)}
.
Together with Remark
5.13
, this corollary implies that
[
S2
, Conjecture 1]
is true
if we invert
\mathrm{char}(k)
in
\mathrm{CH}_{d}(T^{*}Y)
, as we promised in the introduction.

Appendix A
Functoriality of
\infty
-topoi

A.1

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-topos (cf.
[
HTT
, 6.1.0.4]
), and
\mathcal{D}
be an
\infty
-category.
Recall from
[
SAG
, 1.3.1.4]
that the
\infty
-category of
\mathcal{D}
-valued sheaves denoted by
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})

is the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})
spanned by limit preserving functors.
For an
\infty
-site
\mathcal{C}
(
i.e.
small
\infty
-categories endowed with Grothendieck topology
[
HTT
, 6.2.2.1]
),
we may define the notion of
\mathcal{D}
-valued sheaves on
\mathcal{C}
by descent property (cf.
[
SAG
, 1.3.1.1]
)
and this forms an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{C})
.
The
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C}))
and
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{C})
do not coincide in general,
but if
\mathcal{D}
admits small limits, these two
\infty
-categories coincide by
[
SAG
, 1.3.1.7]
.
In this paper, we talk about
\mathcal{D}
-valued sheaves exclusively in the case where
\mathcal{D}
is presentable.
In particular, in that case,
\mathcal{D}
admits small limits by
[
HTT
, 5.5.2.4]
.
For this reason, we identify the above two
\infty
-categories without further mentioning.

A.2

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-site.
Instead of saying that a subcategory
\mathrm{R}\subset\mathcal{C}_{/C}
is a sieve of
C
(cf.
[
HTT
, 6.2.2.1]
),
we often say that
\mathrm{R}\rightarrow C
is a sieve for short.
Let us recall a well-known construction of geometric morphisms of
\infty
-topoi.

Definition
. —

Let
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
be
\infty
-sites.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a functor.
The morphism
f
is said to be
continuous
if any covering sieve
\mathrm{R}\rightarrow C
of
C\in\mathcal{C}
,
the sieve of
f(C)
generated by
\{f(r)\rightarrow f(C)\}_{r\in\mathrm{R}}
is a covering sieve in
\mathcal{D}
.

Let us recall the following analogues of [SGA 4, Exp. III, 1.6 and 1.3 (5)]:

Lemma
. —

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a continuous morphism between
\infty
-sites.

1.

Assume that
\mathcal{C}
admits pullbacks, and
f
commutes with pullbacks.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Then the functor
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathcal{C})

defined by composing with
f^{\mathrm{op}}
factors through
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathcal{C})
,
and defines a functor
f^{\mathrm{s}}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathcal{C})
.

2.

Assume further that
\mathcal{C}
admits finite limits, and
f
commutes with finite limits.
Then
f^{\mathrm{s}}
admits an exact left adjoint, and defines a geometric morphism
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C})
of
\infty
-topoi.

Proof.

Let us show the first statement.
By a similar argument to the beginning of the proof of
[
SAG
, A.3.3.1]
, it suffices to show the claim when
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
.
This case follows by
[
SAG
, 20.6.1.3]
.
The second claim follows by
[
HTT
, 6.2.3.20]
since the composition
\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D})

commutes with finite limits by
[
HTT
, 5.1.3.2]
.
∎

A.3 Lemma
. —

Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be a geometric morphism of
\infty
-topoi, and let
\mathcal{D}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
The composition with
(f^{*})^{\mathrm{op}}
induces the functor
f^{\mathcal{D}}_{*}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{Y})
,
and it admits a left adjoint
f^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}
. The functors
f^{\mathcal{D}}_{*}
,
f^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}
are often denoted by
f_{*}
,
f^{*}
for simplicity.

Proof.

Consider the following diagram of
\infty
-categories

\textstyle{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}

where
F:=\circ(f^{*})^{\mathrm{op}}
, and
G
is a left Kan extension whose existence is assured by
[
HTT
, 4.3.2.15]

and the assumption that
\mathcal{D}
is presentable.
Thus, by
[
HTT
, 4.3.3.7]
,
G
is left adjoint to
F
.
Now, since
f^{*}
preserves small colimits, its opposite
(f^{*})^{\mathrm{op}}
preserves small limits.
This implies that the essential image of
F\circ\iota_{\mathcal{X}}
is contained in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{Y})
, and yields the desired functor
f^{\mathcal{D}}_{*}
.
The functor
\iota_{\mathcal{X}}
admits a left adjoint
L_{\mathcal{X}}
by
[
SAG
, 1.3.4.3]
.
The composition
L_{\mathcal{X}}\circ G\circ\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}
is a left adjoint to
f^{\mathcal{D}}_{*}
.
Indeed, given
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
,
\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{Y})
, we have

\mathrm{Map}\bigl(L_{\mathcal{X}}\circ G\circ\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathcal{G}),\mathcal{F}\bigr)\simeq\mathrm{Map}\bigl(\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathcal{G}),\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}f^{\mathcal{D}}_{*}(\mathcal{F})\bigr)

by the adjointness, and since
\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}
is fully faithful, we get the claim.
∎

A.4

We consider the following condition:

(*) Let
\mathcal{A}
be a closed symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category (cf.
[
HA
, 4.2.1.32]
)
and
\mathcal{D}
be an
\infty
-category enriched, left-tensored, and cotensored over
\mathcal{A}
(cf.
[
HA
, 4.2.1.28]
).
Furthermore, we assume that both
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{D}
are compactly generated (cf.
[
HTT
, 5.5.7.1]
), in particular presentable.

Very informally, these mean that we are endowed with left-tensor functor
\otimes\colon\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}

(as well as great amount of homotopy coherence data),
and for
a\in\mathcal{A}
,
d,d^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}
, the morphism object
\mathrm{Mor}(d,d^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}
and cotensor object
{}^{a}d\in\mathcal{D}
are defined.
Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-topos.
By
[
HA
, 2.1.3.4]
,
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})
is left-tensored over
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{A})
.
This left-tensor structure descends to a left-tensor structure for sheaves as follows:

Lemma
. —

For an
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{X}
, the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X})
is closed symmetric monoidal,
and
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
is an
\infty
-category enriched, left-tensored, and cotensored over
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X})
.

Proof.

We borrow the argument from
[
SAG
, 1.3.4.6]
.
Let
\otimes\colon\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
denote the left-tensor functor (cf.
[
HA
, 4.2.1.19]
).
The existence of morphism objects and exponential objects implies that
\otimes
commutes with colimits separately in each variable.
By
[
HTT
, 5.5.8.6, 5.5.8.3]
, the functor
\otimes
commutes with small
filtered
colimits.
Now, the left-tensor on
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})
is pointwise, and can be described as the composition

\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{A})\times\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})\cong\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\otimes}\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D}),

Note that the product of compactly generated
\infty
-categories remains to be compactly generated by
[
HTT
, 5.5.7.6]
.
By
[
SAG
, 1.3.4.4]
, since we are assuming
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{D}
are compactly generated, this functor preserves
L
-equivalences.
Similarly, the monoidal structure of
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{A})
preserves
L
-equivalence as well.
Thus, as
[
HA
, 2.2.1.7]
, the left-tensor and monoidal structure are compatible with
L
, and
invoking
[
HA
, 2.2.1.9]
, we conclude that
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X})
is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category and

\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
is left-tensored over it.
Moreover,
[
HA
, 2.2.1.9]
implies that for
A\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X})
and
D\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
,
the left-tensor
A\otimes D
is equivalent to
L(\iota(A)\otimes\iota(D))
,
where
\iota\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{-}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},-)
is the inclusion functor.
This presentation implies that the left-tensor
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X})\times\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})

commutes with small colimits separately in each variable.
Since
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
and
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X})
are presentable,
by
[
HA
, 4.2.1.33]
,
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
is cotensored and enriched.
∎

The morphism object functor and exponential object functor are denoted by

\displaystyle\underline{\mathrm{Mor}}_{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X}),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathrm{Cot}}_{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X})^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X}).

Remark
. —

Let us give two examples of compactly generated
\infty
-categories we use in this paper.
First, let
\mathcal{C}
be a compactly generated symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
and the tensor product preserves small colimits separately in each variable.
Then for an algebra object
A
of
\mathcal{C}
, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{RMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
is compactly generated.
In fact, the proof of
[
HA
, 5.3.2.12 (3)]
shows this.

Secondly, let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-topos such that
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(\mathcal{X})
is a compactly generated for some commutative ring
R
.
Let
\mathcal{D}
be a compactly generated
R
-linear
\infty
-category (cf.
[
SAG
, D.1.4.1]
).
Then since
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})\simeq\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(\mathcal{X})\otimes_{R}\mathcal{D}
by
[
SAG
, 1.3.1.6]
,
the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
is a compactly generated
\infty
-category by
[
SAG
, D.5.3.3]
.

A.5 Lemma
. —

We assume
(*)
of
A.4
.
Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-topos.
Let
I
be a simplicial set, and
F\colon I\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X})
,
G\colon I\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
be functors.
For
A\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X})
and
D\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
, we have equivalences

\underline{\mathrm{Cot}}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits(F),D\bigr)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits\underline{\mathrm{Cot}}(F,D),\qquad\underline{\mathrm{Cot}}\bigl(A,\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits(G)\bigr)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits\underline{\mathrm{Cot}}(A,G).

We have similar equivalences also for
\underline{\mathrm{Mor}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show the equivalences after applying
\mathrm{Map}(E,-)
for
E\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
.
This follows by the fact that
\otimes
commutes with small colimits separately in each variable.
∎

A.6 Lemma
. —

We assume
(*)
of
A.4
.
Assume we are given a geometric morphism
f\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
of
\infty
-topoi.
Then
f^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{X})

can be promoted to a monoidal functor and

f^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
is monoidal as well
with respect to the left-tensor structure

(
more precisely
\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
-monoidal using the notation of
[
HA
, 4.2.1.13]
)
.

Proof.

Let
f^{\mathrm{p}}_{\mathcal{D}}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})
,
or
f^{\mathrm{p}}
for short, be the left adjoint of the functor
(\circ f^{*})
.
Recall from the proof of Lemma
A.3
that
f^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}\simeq L_{\mathcal{X}}\circ f^{\mathrm{p}}_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Assume that
D\rightarrow D^{\prime}
is an
L_{\mathcal{Y}}
-equivalence in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})
.
For any
E
in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
, we have

\mathrm{Map}(f^{\mathrm{p}}D^{\prime},E)\simeq\mathrm{Map}(D^{\prime},E\circ f^{*})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Map}(D,E\circ f^{*})\simeq\mathrm{Map}(f^{\mathrm{p}}D,E),

where the second equivalence follows since
E\circ f^{*}
remains to be a sheaf.
By definition (cf.
[
HTT
, 5.5.4.1]
), the localization functor
L
is compatible with
f^{\mathrm{p}}
,
namely
f^{\mathrm{p}}(D)\rightarrow f^{\mathrm{p}}(D^{\prime})
is
L_{\mathcal{X}}
-equivalent.

For
Z\in\{\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}\}
, let
\mathcal{C}_{Z}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
be the coCartesian fibration of

\infty
-operads which exhibits the pointwise left-tensor structure of
\mathrm{Fun}(Z^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})
.
Then we have the localization map
L^{\otimes}_{Z}\colon\mathcal{C}_{Z}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{Z}^{\otimes}
so that
\mathcal{D}_{Z}^{\otimes}

exhibits the left-tensor structure of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(Z)
over
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(Z)
.
Since
L_{Z}^{\otimes}
and the inclusion are maps of
\infty
-operads by
[
HA
, 2.2.1.9]
,
we define a map of
\infty
-operads as the composition

F\colon\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{Y}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{Y}}\xrightarrow{f^{\mathrm{p},\otimes}}\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{L^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{X}}}\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{X}}.

By construction of
f^{*}
in Lemma
A.3
, the underlying functor coincides with
f^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}\times f^{*}_{\mathcal{D}}
.
We must show that this map preserves coCartesian edges (over
\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
) to conclude the proof.
It suffices to show that for
A,A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{Y})
,
D\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{Y})
, the morphisms

F(A\otimes A^{\prime})\rightarrow F(A)\otimes F(A^{\prime}),\qquad F(A\otimes D)\rightarrow F(A)\otimes F(D)

are equivalences.
These follow by the compatibility of
L
and
f^{\mathrm{p}}_{\mathcal{A}}
,
f^{\mathrm{p}}_{\mathcal{D}}
.
∎

Corollary
. —

We assume
(*)
of
A.4
.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be a geometric morphism of
\infty
-topoi.
For
A\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{Y})
and
D\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{X})
, we have canonical equivalences

\underline{\mathrm{Mor}}_{\mathcal{Y}}(A,f^{\mathcal{D}}_{*}D)\xrightarrow{\sim}f^{\mathcal{D}}_{*}\underline{\mathrm{Mor}}_{\mathcal{X}}(f^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}(A),D),\qquad\underline{\mathrm{Cot}}_{\mathcal{Y}}(A,f^{\mathcal{D}}_{*}D)\xrightarrow{\sim}f^{\mathcal{D}}_{*}\underline{\mathrm{Cot}}_{\mathcal{X}}(f^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}(A),D).

Proof.

These follow by the adjointness of the pair
(f^{*}_{\mathcal{D}},f^{\mathcal{D}}_{*})
and the previous lemma.
∎

A.7

We finish this section by introducing a family version of
\infty
-site.

Definition

(cf. [SGA 4, Exp. VI, 7.2.1, 7.4.2])
. —

1.

A
Cartesian
\infty
-site
(8)
(8)

(8)

Strictly speaking, the definition is not compatible with that of SGA even in the case of
1
-topos.
In order to be compatible with that of SGA, we should have assumed the
existence of finite limits for each fiber and finite limits should be preserved by pullbacks.
We could have employed the finite limit version, but to clarify what we need in the discussion, we used pullback version.

is a Cartesian fibration
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
of
\infty
-categories
equipped with Grothendieck topology on
\mathcal{X}_{c}
for each
c\in\mathcal{C}
such that the following holds:
1. for each
c\in\mathcal{C}
, the fiber
\mathcal{X}_{c}
admits pullbacks;
2. for each morphism
c\rightarrow c^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{C}
, the associated functor
\mathcal{X}_{c^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{c}

is continuous with respect to the Grothendieck topologies and commutes with pullbacks.

2.

The
total topology
on
\mathcal{X}
is the topology generated by the coverings
\{\iota_{c}(X_{i})\rightarrow\iota_{c}(X)\}_{i\in I}

such that
\{X_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
is a covering in
\mathcal{X}_{c}
and
\iota_{c}\colon\mathcal{X}_{c}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is the inclusion.

A.8

For a Cartesian
\infty
-site, it is convenient to introduce some generalization of
\mathcal{D}
-valued sheaves.
Let
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be a Cartesian
\infty
-site, and let
p\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
be a presentable fibration.
We denote by
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X}):=\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})
.
Let
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
spanned by functors
\mathcal{F}
such that for any sieve

\mathrm{R}\rightarrow X
in
\mathcal{X}
with respect to the total topology on
\mathcal{X}
, the diagram

(\mathrm{R}^{\mathrm{op}})^{\triangleleft}\cong(\mathrm{R}^{\triangleright})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{D}

is a
p
-limit diagram.

Lemma
. —

1.

The
\infty
-category
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
is stable if each fiber of
p
is stable.

2.

A presheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
is a sheaf if and only if for any
c\in\mathcal{C}
, the fiber
\mathcal{F}|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}_{c}}

is a
\mathcal{D}_{c}
-valued sheaf on
\mathcal{X}_{c}
.

3.

There exists a localization functor
L\colon\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
.

Proof.

The
\infty
-category
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
is stable by
[
HA
, 1.1.4.5]

(applied to
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D}\times_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}})
).
Let us show
1
.
It suffices to show that given a morphism
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
,
the cofiber computed in
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
is a sheaf.
Let
\mathcal{H}
be the cofiber in
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
.
It suffices to show that
p\mbox{-}\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow p\mbox{-}\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{G}\rightarrow p\mbox{-}\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{H}
,
where
p\mbox{-}\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits
denotes the
p
-limit and
\mathrm{R}\rightarrow X
is a covering sieve, is a cofiber sequence in
\mathcal{D}_{X}
.
Since
\mathcal{D}_{X}
is stable, this is equivalent to showing that the sequence is a fiber sequence.
Since the formation of fiber sequence commutes with
p
-limits, it suffices to show that the sequence
\mathcal{F}(Y)\rightarrow\mathcal{G}(Y)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}(Y)

is a fiber sequence for any
Y\in\mathcal{X}
, which follows since the sequence is a cofiber sequence in
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
.

Let us show
2
.
First, let us show the claim in the case
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{0}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
for some presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}_{0}
.
In this case,
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})\simeq\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}(\mathcal{X})
.
Assume
\mathcal{F}
is a
\mathcal{D}_{0}
-valued sheaf.
Since the functor
\mathcal{X}_{c}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is continuous and commutes with pullbacks,

\mathcal{F}|_{\mathcal{X}_{c}^{\mathrm{op}}}
is a sheaf by Lemma
A.2
.
Assume
\mathcal{F}
is a
\mathcal{D}_{0}
-valued presheaf such that the restriction to
\mathcal{X}_{c}
is a sheaf for each
c\in\mathcal{C}
.
Now, an obvious
\infty
-categorical analogue of [SGA 4, Exp. II, 2.2] for
\mathcal{D}_{0}
-valued presheaves hold.
Since the formation of the generating families
\{\iota_{c}(X_{i})\rightarrow\iota_{c}(X)\}_{i\in I}

is stable under pullbacks by the assumption that the associated functor

\mathcal{X}_{c^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{c}
commutes with pullbacks, it suffices to show the Čech descent for the generating families.
This follows since the functor
\iota_{c}\colon\mathcal{X}_{c}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
commutes with pullbacks.

Let us handle the general case.
Assume we are given a
\mathcal{D}
-valued presheaf
\mathcal{F}
so that
\mathcal{F}|_{\mathcal{X}_{c}}
is a
\mathcal{D}_{c}
-valued sheaf for any
c
.
Let
\mathrm{R}\rightarrow X
be a sieve, and we wish to show that the diagram

(\mathrm{R}^{\mathrm{op}})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is a
p
-limit diagram.
Let
c\in\mathcal{C}
be the image of
X
.
By replacing
\mathcal{C}
by
\mathcal{C}_{/c}
, which does not affect the
p
-limit since
\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
is presentable,
we may assume that
c
is a final object in
\mathcal{C}
.
Consider the functor
\mathcal{D}\times\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
sending
\mathcal{D}\times\{0\}
to
c
and

\mathcal{D}\times\{1\}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
is
p
.
We consider the
p
-right Kan extension of
\mathrm{id}\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
along the inclusion

\mathcal{D}\times\{1\}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}\times\Delta^{1}
.
By restricting the Kan extension to
\mathcal{D}\times\{0\}
, we have a functor

\alpha\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{c}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}

of presentable fibrations over
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Informally, this is the functor sending
d\in\mathcal{D}
to
F(d)
where
F\colon\mathcal{D}_{p(d)}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{c}
is the associated functor.
It suffices to show that
(\mathrm{R}^{\mathrm{op}})^{\triangleleft}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{D}\xrightarrow{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{c}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}

is a
p
-limit diagram by
[
HTT
, 4.3.1.9]
.
For this, it suffices to show that
\alpha\circ\mathcal{F}
is a
\mathcal{D}_{c}
-valued sheaf.
By what we have shown, we must show that
(\alpha\circ\mathcal{F})|_{\mathcal{X}_{c^{\prime}}}
is a sheaf for any
c^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}
.
Since
\alpha_{c^{\prime}}\colon\mathcal{D}_{c^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{c}
commutes with limits, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{F}|_{\mathcal{X}_{c^{\prime}}}
is a sheaf,
but this is the assumption.
The other direction can be checked similarly.

Let us show
3
.
For
c\in\mathcal{C}
, consider the restriction functor
r_{c}\colon\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{c}}(\mathcal{X}_{c})
.
By considering the
p
-left Kan extension of
\mathcal{X}_{c}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{c}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}

along
\mathcal{X}_{c}^{\mathrm{op}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
,
r_{c}
admits a left adjoint by
[
HTT
, 4.3.2.16]
.
Let
\mathcal{S}_{c}
be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
spanned by objects
\mathcal{F}

such that
r_{c}(\mathcal{F})
belongs to
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}_{c}}(\mathcal{X}_{c})
.
By
[
HTT
, 5.5.4.17]
,
\mathcal{S}_{c}
is strongly reflective.
By
2
, we have
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})=\bigcap_{c\in\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{S}_{c}
,
thus by
[
HTT
, 5.5.4.18]
,
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X})
is strongly reflective as well, and the existence of
L
follows.
∎

Appendix B
Family of morphism objects and end calculus

B.1

Let
p\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be a coCartesian fibration.
Let
\tau\colon\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
be the functor sending
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
We have the coCartesian fibration
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}:=\mathrm{Un}(\tau\circ\mathrm{St}(p))\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
.
This is a coCartesian fibration whose fiber over
X\in\mathcal{C}
is
\mathcal{D}_{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}
depends on
p
, the notation is ambiguous, and we use the notation unless any confusion may arise.
The construction is functorial for functors which preserve coCartesian edges.
Namely, if we are given a functor
F\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
which preserves coCartesian edges,
we have the induced functor
\widetilde{F}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
.
Caution that if
F
does not preserve coCartesian edges, we generally do not have the induced functor.
We also have a similar construction for Cartesian fibrations.
If
q\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
is a Cartesian fibration, we denote by
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}

the Cartesian fibration whose fiber over any
c\in\mathcal{C}
is
q^{-1}(C)^{\mathrm{op}}
constructed similarly.
By construction, we have
(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}})^{\mathrm{op}}\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{op}}}
for a coCartesian fibration
\mathcal{D}
over
\mathcal{C}
.

Some models of
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}
are known, but let us recall that of Lurie from
[
SAG
, 14.4.2]
.
Let
f\colon X\rightarrow S
be a map of simplicial sets.
We put
\mathrm{Dl}(f):=f_{*}(X\times\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
(cf.
2.1
), which we also denote by
\mathrm{Dl}(X)
.
The fiber of
\mathrm{Dl}(f)
over
s\in S
is
\mathrm{Fun}(X_{s},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})\cong\mathcal{P}(X_{s}^{\mathrm{op}})
.
The simplicial set
\mathrm{Dl}^{0}(f)
is the full simplicial subset of
\mathrm{Dl}(f)
spanned by vertices of the form

\mathrm{Map}(x,-)\colon X_{s}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
for some
s\in S
and
x\in X_{s}
.
In other words, vertices of
\mathrm{Dl}^{0}(f)
correspond to corepresentable functors.
If
f
is a coCartesian fibration,
\mathrm{Dl}^{0}(f)
and
\mathrm{Dl}(f)
are coCartesian fibrations as well by
[
SAG
, 14.4.2.3]
.
By
[
SAG
, 14.4.2.4]
(9)
(9)

(9)

We think that the claim of the reference contains typos, and
q
in
[
SAG
, 14.4.2.4]
should be a coCartesian fibration not a Cartesian fibration.
Indeed, if we are given a Cartesian fibration
q\colon X\rightarrow S
, the map
\mathrm{Dl}^{0}(p)\rightarrow S
may no longer be Cartesian.
To see this, consider a presentable fibration
q\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}
whose associated left adjoint
F\colon\mathcal{C}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{1}

does
not
admit a left adjoint.
By
[
SAG
, 14.4.2.3 (4)]
,
\mathrm{Dl}^{0}(q)\rightarrow S
is a coCartesian fibration.
If it were a Cartesian fibration as well, then
F^{\mathrm{op}}
would admit a right adjoint, which would imply that
F
admits a left adjoint.
This is a contradiction.

,

\mathrm{Dl}^{0}(f)
is categorically equivalent to
\widetilde{X}
.

Now, let
\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be coCartesian fibrations,
and assume we are given a functor
t\colon\mathcal{D}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
.
By definition, this functor is equivalent to giving a functor
t^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathrm{Dl}(\mathcal{D})
.
Assume the following condition holds:

For each
e\in\mathcal{E}
, the functor
t(-,e)\colon\mathcal{D}_{t(e)}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
is corepresentable.

Then
t^{\prime}
factors through
\mathrm{Dl}^{0}(\mathcal{D})
.
Note that the above condition can be checked fiberwise.

B.2

We will perform a relative version of the construction of
[
HA
, 4.2.1.31]
.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category, and let
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
,
p\colon\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be coCartesian fibrations.
Assume we are given a functor
t\colon\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
over
\mathcal{C}
which preserves coCartesian edges.
The functor
t
is called the
left-tensor functor relative to
\mathcal{C}
.
Because of this preservation property, we may consider
\widetilde{t}
.
This induces the functor

\tau\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\simeq(\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M})^{\sim}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{t}\times\mathrm{id}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc},

where the second map is the map corresponding to
\mathcal{M}\simeq\mathrm{Dl}^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Dl}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})
,
or informally, the functor sending
(m,m^{\prime})
to
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{M}}(m,m^{\prime})
.
This induces the functors

t^{\prime}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathrm{Dl}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}),\qquad t^{\prime\prime}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathrm{Dl}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}).

Consider the following conditions:

1.

For any
c\in\mathcal{C}
and
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
, the functor

\tau(-,m,m^{\prime})\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}}\bigl(t(-,m),m^{\prime}\bigr)\colon(\mathcal{A}_{c})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
is corepresentable in
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{op}}_{c}

(equivalently, representable in
\mathcal{A}_{c}
);

2.

For any
c\in\mathcal{C}
and
a\in\mathcal{A}_{c}
,
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
, the functor

\tau(a,-,m)\colon(\mathcal{M}_{c})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
is corepresentable in
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{op}}_{c}
.

If the first condition is satisfied, then
t^{\prime}
induces
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathrm{Dl}^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}})\simeq\mathcal{A}

by the observation in
B.1
.
This functor is denoted by
\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{M}}
.
If the second condition is satisfied, then
t^{\prime\prime}
induces
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathrm{Dl}^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\mathcal{M}
.
This functor is denoted by
\mathrm{Cot}_{\mathcal{M}}
.

Remark
. —

Let
e\colon x\rightarrow y
be an edge in
\mathcal{C}
, and assume we are given
a\in\mathcal{A}_{x}
,
m\in\mathcal{M}_{x}
.
Then the functor
\mathrm{Cot}_{\mathcal{M}}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
induces
the morphism
\widetilde{e}\colon e_{!}\mathrm{Cot}_{\mathcal{M}}(a,m)\rightarrow\mathrm{Cot}_{\mathcal{M}}(e_{!}a,e_{!}m)
where
e_{!}
denotes the coCartesian pushforward along
e
.
This edge can be described up to homotopy equivalence as follows:
Consider the composition of morphisms

t(e_{!}(a)\boxtimes e_{!}\mathrm{Cot}(a,m))\xleftarrow{\sim}e_{!}t(a\boxtimes\mathrm{Cot}(a,m))\rightarrow e_{!}m,

where the first morphism is an equivalence since
t
is assumed to preserve coCartesian edges.
By taking adjoint, this morphism yields
e_{!}\mathrm{Cot}(a,m)\rightarrow\mathrm{Cot}(e_{!}a,e_{!}m)
.
Unwinding the definition, this morphism is homotopic to
\widetilde{e}
.

B.3

For an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, the
\infty
-category of twisted arrows
\mathrm{Tw}\mathcal{C}
is defined in
[
HA
, 5.2.1.1]
.
If we are given a categorical equivalence
F\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
of
\infty
-categories,
the induced functor
\mathrm{Tw}(F)
is also a categorical equivalence.
Indeed, it suffices to show that the induced functor

\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{D})\times_{(\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{op}})}(\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})

is a categorical equivalence, but since both are right fibrations over
\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
by
[
HA
, 5.2.1.3]
,
the verification is fiberwise.
On the other hand, since each fiber is the
\infty
-groupoïd of mapping spaces, the claim follows.
Thus, since
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
is the localization of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{\circ}

(
i.e.
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}
spanned by fibrant and cofibrant objects) with respect to categorical equivalences,

\mathrm{Tw}
induces the functor
\mathrm{Tw}\colon\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
.
Let
p\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be a coCartesian fibration.
We put
\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{D}):=\mathrm{Un}(\mathrm{Tw}\circ\mathrm{St}(p))
, which is the coCartesian fibration over
\mathcal{C}

whose fiber over
c\in\mathcal{C}
is
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{D}_{c})
.
This is equipped with a functor
\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{D}

of coCartesian fibrations over
\mathcal{C}
which preserves coCartesian edges.

Now, let
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be a coCartesian fibration, and assume we are given functors

F\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
,
G\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
over
\mathcal{C}
, and a section
m\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
.
Then this induces a functor

F\boxtimes G\colon\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{F\times G}\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{t}\mathcal{M}.

Let
\mathrm{tw}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be the canonical functor.
The
space of extranatural transform
is defined by

\mathrm{Ex}(F\times G,m):=\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})}\bigl(F\boxtimes G,m\circ\mathrm{tw}\bigr)

A vertex of the space, an extranatural transform, is denoted by
F\times G\xrightarrow[\bullet]{}m
.

The
coend of
F\times G
relative to
\mathcal{C}
is the
p
-left Kan extension of
F\boxtimes G

along the morphism
\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
, and denoted by
F\otimes G\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
.
By definition, we have the equivalence

\mathrm{Ex}(F\times G,m)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{M})}(F\otimes G,m).

B.4

From now on, we assume the following:

The coCartesian fibrations
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
are presentable fibrations,
and the left-tensor commutes with colimits in each variable fiberwise.

Let us fix a functor
G\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
.
Then we have the functors

\mathrm{RT}_{G}\colon\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{M}),\qquad\mathrm{LT}_{F}\colon\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{M}),

sending
F
to
F\otimes G
and sending
G
to
F\otimes G
.
Let us show that these functors admit right adjoints.
We only treat the case of
\mathrm{RT}_{G}
since the existence of an adjoint of
\mathrm{LT}_{F}
can be shown similarly.
First,
\mathrm{RT}_{G}
commutes with arbitrary colimits.
Indeed, since
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
is assumed to be presentable and in particular a Cartesian fibration, colimits in

\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}},\mathcal{A})\cong\mathrm{Fun}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}},\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})

can be computed pointwise by
[
HTT
, 5.1.2.2]
.
Similarly, colimits in
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
can also be computed pointwise.
Thus, the functor
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
,
sending
F\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
to the composition

\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{F\times G}\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{t}\mathcal{M}
,
commutes with colimits since the left-tensor functor commutes with colimits separately in each variable by assumption.
Since the left Kan extension functor commutes with colimits by (obvious relative version of)
[
HTT
, 4.3.3.7]
,
\mathrm{RT}_{G}
commutes with colimits.
By
[
HTT
, 5.5.3.17]
, both
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}},\mathcal{A})
and
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{M})
are presentable.
Thus, by adjoint functor theorem
[
HTT
, 5.5.2.9]
,
\mathrm{RT}_{G}
admits a right adjoint.
Let us denote this adjoint by
M_{G}
.
The right adjoint of
\mathrm{LT}_{F}
is denoted by
{}^{F}(\cdot)
.

Now, assume
G
preserves coCartesian edges.
Then, we have the functor

\mathrm{Mor}(G,m)\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{G}\times m}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{M}}}\mathcal{A}.

Similarly, if
F
preserves coCartesian edges, we have

\mathrm{Cot}(F,m)\colon\mathcal{E}\cong\mathcal{E}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{F}\times m}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Cot}_{\mathcal{M}}}\mathcal{M}.

Lemma
. —

Assume further that
\mathcal{C}=\Delta^{0}
.
Then we have canonical equivalences
M_{G}(m)\simeq\mathrm{Mor}(G,m)
and
{}^{F}m\simeq\mathrm{Cot}(F,m)
.

Proof.

Since the proof for the first and the second equivalence are similar, we only show the first one.
We must show that there is a canonical equivalence

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{A})}\bigl(F,\mathrm{Mor}(G,m)\bigr)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{M}}(F\otimes G,m).

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{A})}\bigl(F,\mathrm{Mor}(G,m)\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{(x\rightarrow y)\in\mathrm{Tw}^{\mathrm{op}}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}})}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{M}}\bigl(F(x),\mathrm{Mor}(G,m)(y)\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{(x\rightarrow y)\in\mathrm{Tw}^{\mathrm{op}}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}})}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{M}}\bigl(F(x)\otimes G(y),m\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{M}}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{(x\rightarrow y)\in\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{op}})}F(x)\otimes G(y),m\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{M}}\bigl(F\otimes G,m\bigr),

where we used
[
GHN
, 5.1]
for the first equivalence.
∎

Remark
. —

We believe that the lemma holds for any
\mathcal{C}
and any
F
,
G
which preserve coCartesian edges over
\mathcal{C}
.
In fact, if we can construct a suitable morphism
\mathrm{Mor}(G,m)\otimes G\rightarrow m
, then by the base change property,
Proposition
B.8
, the verification is reduced to the case
\mathcal{C}=\Delta^{0}
.
We have just checked the equivalence in this case.
We will show various properties of
M_{G}
and
{}^{F}(-)
that we use in the main text,
but these properties immediately follow if we can show the above lemma for general
\mathcal{C}
.

B.5

It is a nice occasion to record the following straightforwards corollary of
[
GHN
, 5.1]
:

Lemma
. —

Let
\mathcal{E}
,
\mathcal{D}
be
\infty
-categories, let
F,G,H\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be functors,
and let
\alpha\colon F\rightarrow G
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})
.
Assume that for any objects
x,y
in
\mathcal{E}
, the induced morphism

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{D}}\bigl(G(x),H(y)\bigr)\rightarrow\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{D}}\bigl(F(x),H(y)\bigr)
is an equivalence.
Then the induced morphism
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})}(G,H)\rightarrow\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})}(F,H)

is an equivalence.

B.6 Lemma
. —

Let
K
be a simplicial set and
F,G\colon K\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
be functors.
Let
X\rightarrow K
be the left fibration classified by
F
.
Then we have a canonical equivalence

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(K,\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}(F,G)\simeq\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits\bigl(X\rightarrow K\xrightarrow{G}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}\bigr).

Proof.

By definition, we have
\mathrm{Un}(F)\simeq X
.
By
[
HTT
, 2.2.1.2]
, we have

\mathrm{Map}(F,G)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{K}(\mathrm{Un}(F),\mathrm{Un}(G))\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Un}(F)}\bigl(\mathrm{Un}(F),\mathrm{Un}(G)\times_{K}\mathrm{Un}(F)\bigr).

Invoking
[
HTT
, 3.3.3.4]
, we get the lemma.
∎

B.7

In order to facilitate the presentation, we use end/coend notation.
Assume we are given a functor
F\colon\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
between
\infty
-categories.
We define

\int_{c\in\mathcal{C}}F(c,c):=\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits\bigl(\mathrm{Tw}^{\mathrm{op}}\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{D}\bigr),\quad\int^{c\in\mathcal{C}}F(c,c):=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits\bigl(\mathrm{Tw}\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{D}\bigr)

and call them the
end
and
coend
respectively.
Even though the notation is slightly ambiguous, we do not think it causes any confusion.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category admitting colimits.
Then
\mathcal{C}
is left tensored over
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
by
[
HTT
, 4.4.4]
.
For
C\in\mathcal{C}
and
X\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
, the left tensor is denoted by
X\cdot C
.
The following crucial results are
\infty
-categorical analogues of well-known properties of coend in the ordinary category theory:

Lemma
. —

Let
F\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a functor between
\infty
-categories, and
G\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be a right fibration.
Then, we have a canonical equivalence

\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{X}}(F\circ G)\simeq\int^{c\in\mathcal{C}}G^{-1}(c)\cdot F(c).

If we are given a map of right fibrations over
\mathcal{C}
, then this equivalence is functorial in an evident manner.

Proof.

For any
y\in\mathcal{C}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle\bigl(\int^{c\in\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{St}(G)(c)\cdot F(c),y\bigr)\simeq\int_{c\in\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}}\bigl(\mathrm{St}(G)(c),\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{D}}(F(c),y)\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}\bigl(\mathrm{St}(G),\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{D}}(F(-),y)\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\underleftarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{D}}(F\circ G(x),y)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{D}}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{X}}F\circ G,y\bigr),

where we used
[
GHN
, 5.1]
for the 2nd equivalence and Lemma
B.6
for the 3rd equivalence.
Given a morphism
y\rightarrow y^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{C}
, the above equivalence is functorial.
Thus, the lemma follows.
∎

Corollary

(co-Yoneda lemma)
. —

Let
F\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a functor between
\infty
-categories.
Then for any
x\in\mathcal{C}
, we have a canonical equivalence

F(x)\simeq\int^{c\in\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(c,x)\cdot F(c).

Proof.

Apply the previous lemma to the right fibration
\mathcal{C}_{/x}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
.
∎

B.8 Proposition
. —

Let the notation be as in
B.4
.
Assume
G
preserves coCartesian edges.
Let
h\colon\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be a functor, and let
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
be the base changes to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
We also denote by
G^{\prime}
,
m^{\prime}
the base changes of
G
,
m
. Then we have
M_{G}(m)\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\simeq M_{G^{\prime}}(m^{\prime})
.
If
F
preserves coCartesian edges, we have a similar equivalence
{}^{F}m\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\simeq{}^{F^{\prime}}(m^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Since the proof is essentially the same, we only show the claim for
G
.
Let
p_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
.
We have the
p_{\mathcal{A}}
-left Kan extension functor

h_{!}\colon\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})\cong\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}},\mathcal{A}).

Abusing the notation, we denote by
h_{!}
also for the left Kan extension

\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{M})
defined similarly.
The right adjoint, which is merely the restriction to
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
or to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, is denoted by
h^{*}
.
Let
\mathrm{RT}_{G^{\prime}}\colon\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}^{\prime})

be the functor sending
F^{\prime}
to
F^{\prime}\otimes G^{\prime}
.
By adjunction, the proposition is equivalent to showing the equivalence
h_{!}\circ\mathrm{RT}_{G^{\prime}}\simeq\mathrm{RT}_{G}\circ h_{!}
, which we will prove.

By universal property of left Kan extension, we have a morphism
\mathrm{RT}_{G^{\prime}}\rightarrow h^{*}\circ\mathrm{RT}_{G}\circ h_{!}
, and by taking the adjoint,
we have the morphism
h_{!}\circ\mathrm{RT}_{G^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathrm{RT}_{G}\circ h_{!}
.
We wish to show that this is an equivalence.
Since the verification is pointwise, and the construction of the morphism is functorial with respect to the composition of
h
,
it suffices to show the claim for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=\Delta^{0}
.
Furthermore, it suffices to take
F\in\mathrm{Fun}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})

and show that the morphism
\varphi\colon h_{!}\mathrm{RT}_{G^{\prime}}(F)\rightarrow\mathrm{RT}_{G}(h_{!}(F))
is an equivalence in
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{M})
.
Again, this is pointwise, thus, it suffices to show that
\varphi(c)
, regarded as a morphism in
\mathcal{M}
, is equivalent for any
c\in\mathcal{C}
.
Let
c^{\prime}
be the vertex of
\mathcal{C}
determined by
h\colon\Delta^{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
.

To show this, we use some coend calculus.
Let
q\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
.
For
e\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
,
consider the map
\alpha_{e}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{c^{\prime}}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{/e}\rightarrow\{c^{\prime}\}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}_{/c}
,
which is a coCartesian fibration by
[
HTT
, 2.4.3.2]
.
For
a\in\mathcal{A}
,
m\in\mathcal{M}
, we denote
t(a,m)
by
a\otimes m
, and if we are given an edge
a
of
\mathcal{C}
,
a^{*}
denotes the coCartesian pushforward.
Then we can compute

\displaystyle\mathrm{RT}_{G}h_{!}(F)(c)

\displaystyle\underset{(1)}{\simeq}\int^{e\in\mathcal{E}_{c}}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{g\colon e^{\prime}\rightarrow e\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{c^{\prime}}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{/e}}q(g)^{*}F(e^{\prime})\bigr)\otimes G(e)

\displaystyle\underset{(2)}{\simeq}\int^{e\in\mathcal{E}_{c}}\bigl(\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{f\in\mathcal{C}_{c^{\prime}//c}}\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{g\colon e^{\prime}\rightarrow e\in\alpha_{e}^{-1}(f)}f^{*}F(e^{\prime})\bigr)\otimes G(e)

\displaystyle\underset{(3)}{\simeq}\int^{e\in\mathcal{E}_{c}}\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{f\in\mathcal{C}_{c^{\prime}//c}}\Bigl(\int^{e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}_{c^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{E}_{c}^{\mathrm{op}}}(f^{*}e^{\prime},e)\cdot f^{*}F(e^{\prime})\otimes G(e)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\underset{(4)}{\simeq}\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{f\in\mathcal{C}_{c^{\prime}//c}}\int^{e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}_{c^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{op}}}\int^{e\in\mathcal{E}_{c}}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{E}_{c}^{\mathrm{op}}}(f^{*}e^{\prime},e)\cdot f^{*}F(e^{\prime})\otimes G(e)

\displaystyle\underset{(5)}{\simeq}\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{f\in\mathcal{C}_{c^{\prime}//c}}\int^{e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}_{c^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{op}}}f^{*}F(e^{\prime})\otimes\int^{e\in\mathcal{E}_{c}}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{E}_{c}}(e,f^{*}e^{\prime})\cdot G(e)

\displaystyle\underset{(6)}{\simeq}\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{f\in\mathcal{C}_{c^{\prime}//c}}\int^{e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}_{c^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{op}}}f^{*}F(e^{\prime})\otimes G(f^{*}e^{\prime})

\displaystyle\underset{(7)}{\simeq}\mathop{\underrightarrow{\mathrm{lim}}}\nolimits_{f\in\mathcal{C}_{c^{\prime}//c}}f^{*}\bigl(\int^{e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}_{c^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{op}}}F(e^{\prime})\otimes G(e^{\prime})\bigr)\simeq h_{!}\mathrm{RT}_{G^{\prime}}(F)(c).

Here, (1) follows by
[
HTT
, 4.3.1.9, 4.3.1.10]
, (2) follows since
\alpha_{e}
is a coCartesian fibration,
(3) follows by applying Lemma
B.7
to the right fibration
\alpha_{e}^{-1}(f)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{c^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{op}}
,
(4) is by some commutations of colimits, (6) follows by co-Yoneda lemma,
and (7) follows since
G
preserves coCartesian edges and the coCartesian fibration is presentable.
For (3) and (5), we used the assumption that the left-tensor commute with colimits separately in each variable.
We leave the reader to check that the map coincides with
\varphi(c)
.
∎

B.9

Now, let us show some additional properties of
M_{G}
and
{}^{F}(-)
.

Corollary
. —

Assume
G
preserves coCartesian edges.

1.

The canonical map
M_{\mathrm{id}}(m)\circ\widetilde{G}\rightarrow M_{G}(m)
is an equivalence.
In particular, if we are given a functor
f\colon\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
of coCartesian fibrations over

\mathcal{C}
such that
f
preserves coCartesian edges,
we have an equivalence
M_{G}(m)\circ\widetilde{f}\simeq M_{G\circ f}(m)
.

2.

Assume
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}_{0}\times\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{0}\times\mathcal{C}
.
Let
d\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
m\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{0}
be a functor.
Let
G:=\{d\}\times\mathrm{id}\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
and
m^{\prime}:=m\times\mathrm{id}\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
.
Then
M_{G}(m^{\prime})\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}}\mathcal{A}_{0}
is equivalent to the composition

\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\{d\}\times m}\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal{M}_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Mor}}\mathcal{A}_{0}
.

3.

For any edge
f\colon x\rightarrow y
of
\mathcal{E}
, the morphism
M_{G}(m)(x)\rightarrow M_{G}(m)(y)
is homotopic to
the morphism
\mathrm{Mor}(G,m)(x)\rightarrow\mathrm{Mor}(G,m)(y)
in
\mathcal{A}
.

When
F
preserves coCartesian edges, we also have similar results for
{}^{F}m
.

Proof.

Let us show the first claim. By taking the adjoint to the identity
M_{\mathrm{id}}(m)\rightarrow M_{\mathrm{id}}(m)
,
we have the map
M_{\mathrm{id}}(m)\boxtimes\mathrm{id}\rightarrow m\circ\mathrm{tw}_{\mathcal{M}}
of functors

\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
.
By composing with the map
\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}(G)\colon\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathrm{Tw}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}
,
we have the map
M_{\mathrm{id}}(m)\circ\widetilde{G}\boxtimes G\rightarrow m\circ\mathrm{tw}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
The adjoint of this map is the morphism in the statement.
We wish to show that this morphism is an equivalence.
Since the verification is pointwise, and the formation is compatible with base change by the proposition,
it suffices to show the claim for
\mathcal{C}=\Delta^{0}
.
In this case, Lemma
Lemma
implies the equivalence.
The second claim can be shown similarly.

Let us show the last claim.
By the proposition, we may assume that
\mathcal{C}=\Delta^{1}
.
If
f
is over
\Delta^{0}
, then the claim follows by Lemma
Lemma
.
Thus, we may assume that
f
is a coCartesian edge.
In this case, by
1
, we may even assume that
\mathcal{E}=\Delta^{1}
.
Under this situation, let us construct a morphism of functors
\mathrm{Mor}(G,m)\otimes G\rightarrow m
.
Let
e
be the unique non-degenerate edge of
\mathcal{E}=\Delta^{1}
.
Consider the following homotopy commutative diagram in
\mathcal{M}
:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Mor}\bigl(G(0),m(0)\bigr)\otimes G(0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{m(0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{e^{*}\mathrm{Mor}\bigl(G(0),m(0)\bigr)\otimes e^{*}G(0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{e^{*}m(0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mor}\bigl(e^{*}G(0),e^{*}m(0)\bigr)\otimes e^{*}G(0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{e^{*}m(0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\mathrm{Mor}\bigl(e^{*}G(0),m(1)\bigr)\otimes e^{*}G(0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{m(1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mor}\bigl(G(1),m(1)\bigr)\otimes G(1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{m(1).}

Here, the commutativity of the first square follows by functoriality of
e^{*}
,
the second by taking the adjunction of the map
e^{*}\mathrm{Mor}(G(0),m(0))\rightarrow\mathrm{Mor}(e^{*}G(0),e^{*}m(0))
,
the third by functoriality of
\mathrm{Mor}
, and the last by the assumption that
G
preserves coCartesian edges.
Thus we get the desired map.
By taking the adjoint, we have the morphism
\mathrm{Mor}(G,m)\rightarrow M_{G}(m)
.
It remains to show that this is an equivalence.
Since the verification is pointwise, it suffices to show the equivalence for each fiber.
By the base change, it suffices to show the equivalence in the case
\mathcal{C}=\Delta^{0}
, which we have already checked.
∎

B.10

We finish this section with small observations.
Let
\alpha\colon\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
be a map of coCartesian fibrations over
\mathcal{C}
.
Assume that
\mathcal{D}
,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
are left-tensored over
\mathcal{A}
with the left-tensor functors
t_{\mathcal{D}}
,
t_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
,
and satisfy the conditions in
B.2
,
B.4
.
Assume further that
\alpha
is compatible with the left-tensor structure, namely

\alpha\circ t_{\mathcal{D}}\simeq t_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{A}}\times\alpha)
.
Now, assume we are given a map
G\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
of coCartesian fibrations over
\mathcal{C}
.
Under this situation, by definition, we have the morphism
M_{G}(-)\rightarrow M_{\alpha\circ G}(\alpha\circ(-))
in

\mathrm{Fun}\bigl(\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}),\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}},\mathcal{A})\bigr)
.
Assume
\alpha
admits a right adjoint
\beta
relative to
\mathcal{C}
in the sense of
[
HA
, 7.3.2.2]
.
Note that then
\alpha
preserves coCartesian edges by
[
HA
, 7.3.2.6]
.
Then we have the morphism in
\mathrm{Fun}(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})

M_{G}\bigl(\beta(-)\bigr)\rightarrow M_{\alpha\circ G}\bigl(\alpha\circ\beta(-)\bigr)\rightarrow M_{\alpha\circ G}(-)

using the adjunction morphism
\alpha\circ\beta\rightarrow\mathrm{id}
.
Assume further that
G
preserves coCartesian edges.
Then by Proposition
B.8
, the fiber of this morphism over each vertex of
\mathcal{C}
is an equivalence.
Thus we have the equivalence of functors
M_{G}(\beta(-))\simeq M_{\alpha\circ G}(-)
.

B.11

Let us make a small variant of the observation of the last paragraph.
Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\Delta^{0}
.
Assume we are given a functor
f\colon\pi^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
between coCartesian fibrations over
\mathcal{C}
which preserves coCartesian edges.
Assume that both coCartesian fibrations are left-tensored over
\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}
relative to
\mathcal{C}
.
Consider the functor
\widetilde{f}^{\mathrm{op}}\colon\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{op}}

between
Cartesian
fibrations over
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Assume that
\widetilde{f}^{\mathrm{op}}
admits a left adjoint
g^{\mathrm{op}}
relative to
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
By
[
HA
, 7.3.2.6]
, this is equivalent to the existence of left adjoint of
(\widetilde{f}^{\mathrm{op}})_{c}\simeq f_{c}

for each vertex
c
of
\mathcal{C}
.
Now, assume we are given a functor
G\colon\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
which preserves coCartesian edges and
m\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.
As in the previous paragraph, if
g
commutes with left-tensor structures,
we have an equivalence
M_{G}(f\circ\pi^{*}m)\simeq M_{\pi\circ g\circ\widetilde{G}}(m)
in
\mathrm{Fun}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}},\mathcal{A})
.

Appendix C
Enhanced cohomological operations and motivic cohomology

C.1

Let
R
be a commutative ring, and
S
be a noetherian scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
We fix an
(\infty,2)
-functor

\mathbf{D}^{*}_{!}\colon\mathbf{Corr}(S)^{\mathrm{prop}}_{\mathrm{sep};\mathrm{all}}\rightarrow\mathbf{LinCat}^{\mathrm{2-op}}_{R}

which enhances a motivic category of coefficients (cf.
[
A1
, 6.3]
for the details),
and
\mathbf{D}^{*}_{!}(T)
is a
compactly generated
symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category for each
T\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
We do not try to recall the definition of these terminologies, but informally, this means that we have an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant 6-functor formalism.
In particular, for any
T\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
, an
R
-linear
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}_{T}
is associated, and given a morphism
f\colon T\rightarrow T^{\prime}
,
we have functors
f_{*},f_{!}\colon\mathcal{D}_{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{T^{\prime}}
and
f^{*},f^{!}\colon\mathcal{D}_{T^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{T}
with usual adjointness properties.
We have a morphism of functors
f_{!}\rightarrow f_{*}
and this is an equivalence if
f
is proper.
If we are given a closed immersion
i\colon Z\hookrightarrow T
, let
j\colon T\setminus Z\hookrightarrow T
be the complement.
Then we have the localization cofiber sequence
j_{!}j^{*}\rightarrow\mathrm{id}\rightarrow i_{*}i^{*}
in
\mathcal{D}_{T}
.
By taking the underlying
(\infty,1)
-functor of
\mathbf{D}^{*}_{!}
, we, in particular, have a functor
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}

sending
T\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
to
\mathcal{D}_{T}
and
f\colon T\rightarrow T^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
to
f^{*}
.
By unstraightening this functor, we have a coCartesian fibration
\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Since
f^{*}
admits the right adjoint
f_{*}
, this coCartesian fibration is a Cartesian fibration as well by
[
HTT
, 5.2.2.5]
.
Since the fibers are presentable, the fibration is in fact a presentable fibration.
Let
\mathcal{L}\mathrm{in}\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{R}
be the
\infty
-category of
R
-linear
\infty
-categories, or more precisely the underlying
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{LinCat}_{R}

(which is equivalent to
\mathrm{RMod}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}^{\mathbb{A}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})
by
[
A1
, 3.11]
using the notation therein).
Since the fibration is deduced from a functor
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}\mathrm{in}\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{R}
,
\mathcal{D}
is left-tensored over
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.
By definition, morphisms of
\mathbf{LinCat}_{R}
commute with the left-tensor.
Thus, the left-tensor
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\times\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
preserves coCartesian edges.
Since any functor which belongs to
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
commutes with small colimits by definition,
the left-tensor commutes with small colimits separately in each variable.
