In particular, the presentable fibrations

\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}
and
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\times\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}

satisfy the requirements of
B.2
and
B.4
, and we may apply results of the previous section.

Example
. —

Either of the following cohomology theories satisfy the requirements of this paragraph.

1.

Let
R
be a torsion ring in which
p
is invertible.
Then the derived categories of (étale) constructible
R
-modules form a motivic category of coefficients and it admits an enhancement.
See
[
A1
, 6.6]
for a detail.

2.

For a separated noetherian scheme of finite dimension
S
over
k
, Voevodsky constructed the stable homotopy
\infty
-category
\mathcal{SH}(S)
.
For a commutative ring
R
, he also constructed the “motivic Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum”
\mathbb{H}R_{S}
, which is an algebra object in
\mathcal{SH}(S)
.
The categories
\mathcal{SH}(S)
admit a six functor formalism, and in particular, if we are given a morphism
f\colon T\rightarrow S
,
we may consider the pullback
\mathbb{H}R_{T/S}:=f^{*}\mathbb{H}R_{S}
.
We put
\mathcal{D}_{T}:=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{H}R_{T/S}}(\mathcal{SH}(T))
.
Since
\mathcal{SH}(T)
is compactly generated (cf.
[
HA
, 1.4.4.3]
), so is
\mathcal{D}_{T}
by Remark
A.4
.
These categories are organized into a six functor formalism.
The construction is due essentially to Voevodsky, Ayoub, Cisinski-Déglise, and Gaitsgory-Rozenblyum,
and the details as well as references can be found in
[
A1
, 6.8]
.
In particular, if
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
where
k
is a perfect field and
\mathrm{char}(k)
is invertible in
R
,
then the associated Borel-Moore homology (of certain degree)
(10)
(10)

(10)

In general, Borel-Moore homology should coincide with Bloch’s higher Chow group,
but we could not find a reference.

coincides with Chow group (see, for example,
[
A2
, 3.1]
).

C.2

Assume we are given a cohomology theory as in the previous paragraph.
Let
a\colon\mathbb{P}^{1}_{S}\rightarrow S
is the projection.
The unit object of
\mathcal{D}_{T}
is denoted by
R_{T}
for
T\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
We put
R_{S}\!\left<1\right>:=\mathrm{cofib}\bigl(R_{S}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{adj}_{a}}a_{*}a^{*}R_{S}\bigr)
.
Note that
R_{S}\!\left<n\right>\simeq R_{S}(n)[2n]
using the Tate twist.
We put
R_{S}\!\left<n\right>:=(R_{S}\!\left<1\right>)^{\otimes n}
for
n\geq 0
.
For
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{T}
for
b\colon T\rightarrow S
, we put
\mathcal{F}\!\left<n\right>:=\mathcal{F}\otimes b^{*}R\!\left<n\right>
.

We may extract a fundamental functor.
Recall the category
\mathrm{Ar}=\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{S}
from
2.5
.
In
[
A1
, 5.19]
, we constructed a map
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
of coCartesian fibrations over

\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
which preserves coCartesian edges.
Informally, this functor associates to a morphism
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathrm{Ar}
the object
f_{!}f^{*}R_{Y}
.
The construction of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}
is involved since we need the full strength of Gaitsgory-Rozenblyum’s formalism,
and is one of the main theme of
[
A1
]
.

C.3

We assume that the cohomology theory is
oriented
in the sense of Cisinski-Déglise,
more precisely, for any
X\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
, we are given a map (of sets)

\mathrm{Pic}(X)\rightarrow\pi_{0}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(R_{X},R_{X}\!\left<1\right>)
functorial in
X
, called the
orientation
.
If we are given an orientation, for a smooth morphism
f
of relative dimension
d
in
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
,
we have a functorial equivalence
\tau_{f}\colon f^{!}\simeq f^{*}\!\left<d\right>
.
Now, we put
\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}\!\left<n\right>:=M_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\!\left<n\right>}(R)\colon\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}

using the notation of
B.4
.
This is the
bivariant homology functor
.
Since
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}
preserves coCartesian edges, this functor sends
X\rightarrow Y
to

\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(X/Y)\!\left<n\right>:=\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{D}_{Y}}\bigl(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(X/Y)\!\left<n\right>,\mathbf{1}_{Y}\bigr)

by Proposition
B.8
, Lemma
Lemma
, and moreover,
Corollary
Corollary
.
3
shows that it serves as an
\infty
-enhancement of the ordinary Borel-Moore homology.
Recall the notations from
2.6
.
The oriented cohomology theory is said to
admit a trace map
if there exists a morphism of functors

z(d)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}\!\left<d\right>
for any integer
d\geq 0
in
\mathrm{Fun}(\widehat{\mathrm{Ar}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})

which is normalized by the following property:

(*) Consider the morphism
z^{\mathrm{SV}}(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}/S,1)\rightarrow z(1)(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}/S)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}/S)\!\left<1\right>
.
The image of
[\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S,\mathrm{red}}]
by this map, considered as a morphism
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}/S)\!\left<1\right>\rightarrow R_{S}
,
is homotopic to the composition

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}/S)\!\left<1\right>\simeq q_{!}q^{*}R_{S}\!\left<1\right>\simeq q_{!}q^{!}R_{S}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{adj}_{q}}R_{S}
,
where the second equivalence is
q_{!}\tau_{q}
.

Example
. —

When
p
is invertible in
R
, the cohomology theories in Example
C.1
admit trace maps.
For the motivic case, this follows from the main result of
[
A2
, 5.4]

because the assumption of
[
A2
, 5.3]
is satisfied for our
\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}
.
For the étale case, this follows by considering
[
A2
, 2.6]
.

C.4

Let
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{orr}
be the category whose objects are the same as those of
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
and a morphism
Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
is a correspondence,
namely a diagram in
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
of the form
Y^{\prime}\leftarrow Y^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow Y
.
We have the canonical functor
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{orr}\rightarrow\mathbf{Corr}(S)^{\mathrm{prop}}_{\mathrm{sep};\mathrm{all}}
.
Now, put
\mathrm{All}:=(\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{all}})^{\wedge}=\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Sch}_{/S})^{\wedge}
using the notation of
2.5
.
Let
\mathrm{All}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\mathrm{orr}
be the functor sending
Y\rightarrow T
to
Y

and the morphism
F\colon(Y\rightarrow T)\rightarrow(Y^{\prime}\rightarrow T^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{All}

to the correspondence
Y^{\prime}\xleftarrow{g}Y^{\prime}_{T^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{f}Y
where
g
is the projection.
Composing with the underlying
(\infty,1)
-functor of
\mathbf{D}^{*}_{!}
, we get a functor
\mathrm{All}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
.
By unstraightening, we have the coCartesian fibration
D\colon\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!}\rightarrow\mathrm{All}^{\mathrm{op}}

of coCartesian fibrations over
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Informally, the fiber over
(Y\rightarrow T)
is
\mathcal{D}_{Y}
, and given a morphism
F
as above, the associated functor is

\mathcal{D}_{Y^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{g^{*}}\mathcal{D}_{Y^{\prime}_{T}}\xrightarrow{f_{!}}\mathcal{D}_{Y}
.

Now, we use the notations of
3.6
.
Let
h\colon X\rightarrow S
be a morphism of finite type between noetherian separated schemes.
Let
\mathcal{U}\colon\widehat{(X/S)}\vphantom{)}_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{All}^{\mathrm{op}}
be the functor sending

to
U/T
,
and let

be the functor sending
(U/T)
to

.
Unwinding the definition, we have the following commutative diagram on the left

\textstyle{\mathcal{U}^{*}\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!}\times\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}^{*}\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!}\times\Delta^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{All}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\Delta^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{\mathrm{All}^{\mathrm{op}},}

\textstyle{\widehat{(X/S)}\vphantom{)}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{zar}}\times\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where

and

.
By taking a

-left Kan extension and restricting to

,
we have the functor
J_{!}\colon\mathcal{U}^{*}\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!}\rightarrow b_{X}^{*}\mathcal{D}
, which preserves coCartesian edges.
The fiber of this map over
U/T\in\widehat{(X/S)}\vphantom{)}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{zar}}
is equivalent to the functor
j_{!}\colon\mathcal{D}_{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}}

where
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X_{T}
is the open immersion.
Thus, invoking
[
HA
, 7.3.2.6]
,
J_{!}
admits a right adjoint
J^{*}
relative to
\widehat{(X/S)}\vphantom{)}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{zar}}
.
The section
R\colon\widehat{(X/S)}\vphantom{)}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{zar}}\rightarrow b_{X}^{*}\mathcal{D}
, sending
(U/T)
to
R_{X_{T}}
, induces the functor

F:=J^{*}\circ R\colon\widehat{(X/S)}\vphantom{)}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{zar}}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}^{*}\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!}
.
Now, consider the right diagram above, where
c
is the functor sending
(U/T,0)
to
U/T
and
(U/T,1)
to
T/T
.
We take a
D
-left Kan extension of the right diagram above.
We restrict this left Kan extension to
\widehat{(X/S)}\vphantom{)}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{zar}}\times\{1\}
, which we denote by

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\colon\widehat{(X/S)}\vphantom{)}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{zar}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!}
.
Informally, this functor sends
U/T
to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(U/T)\in\mathcal{D}_{T}
.
By construction, this functor factors through
\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!}\times_{\mathrm{All}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
,
where
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}\rightarrow\mathrm{All}
sends
T
to
T\rightarrow T
.
Thus, we have

{}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\colon\widehat{(X/S)}\vphantom{)}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{zar}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!}\times_{\mathrm{All}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\simeq\mathcal{D}

This functor preserves coCartesian edges over
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}
.
We finally put

{}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}:=M_{{}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}(R)\colon(X/S)_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}.

C.5

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an object of
\mathcal{D}_{S}
.
Consider the Cartesian fibration
q\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
Assume we are given a functor

between
\infty
-categories.
Recall we have the section

sending

to

.
Consider the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\times\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R\circ F}

Then we denote by

the composition

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{LKE}(R\circ F)|_{\mathcal{C}\times\{1\}}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{op}}_{S})^{\mathrm{op}}\simeq\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{op}}_{S}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\times\{\mathcal{F}\}}\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{op}}_{S}\times\mathcal{D}_{S}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Mor}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}.

Let

be the full subcategory of

spanned by smooth morphisms

,
and let

In this situation,

is defined.
Indeed, for

and

, it suffices to check the existence of a

-coCartesian edge

over

.
This follows by
[
A1
, 1.5]
.

Lemma
. —

Let
h\colon X\rightarrow S
be as before, and let
\omega_{X/S}:=h^{!}R_{S}
in
\mathcal{D}_{X}
.
Let
(X/S)^{\mathrm{open}}_{\mathrm{sm}}
be the full subcategory of
(X/S)_{\mathrm{zar}}
spanned by objects
U/T
such that
T\rightarrow S
is smooth,
and let
\tau\colon(X/S)^{\mathrm{open}}_{\mathrm{sm}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/X}
be the functor sending
U/T
to
U\subset X_{T}\rightarrow X
.
Then we have the equivalence
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\tau,\omega_{X/S})\simeq{}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}|_{(X/S)^{\mathrm{open}}_{\mathrm{sm}}}
.

Remark
. —

The equivalence
\mathrm{h}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\tau,\omega_{X/S})\simeq\mathrm{h}({}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}|_{(X/S)^{\mathrm{open}}_{\mathrm{sm}}})

of functors between the homotopy categories is an elementary exercise.
Indeed, assume we are given
U/T\in(X/S)^{\mathrm{open}}_{\mathrm{sm}}
,
which yields the following diagram

\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{X_{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{S.}

Then
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\tau,\omega_{X/S})(U/T)
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(R_{X},g^{\prime}_{*}j_{*}j^{*}g^{\prime*}(\omega_{X/S}))
.
This is equivalent to

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}_{U}}(R_{U},j^{*}g^{\prime*}f^{!}R_{S})\simeq\mathrm{Hom}(j_{!}R_{U},f^{!}_{T}g^{*}R_{S})\simeq\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(U/T),R_{T})
, where the first equivalence follows since
g
is smooth.
The last one is equivalent to
{}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(U/T)
.

Proof.

We denote
(X/S)^{\mathrm{open}}_{\mathrm{sm}}
by
(X/S)_{\mathrm{sm}}
for short.
Let
\mathcal{C}:=(\mathrm{Sm}_{S})^{\mathrm{op}}
, and
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{C}}:=\mathcal{D}\times_{\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{C}
.
We put
\pi_{K}\colon K\rightarrow\Delta^{0}
and
\pi:=\pi_{\mathcal{C}}
.
The pullback functor induces the functor
f^{*}\colon\pi^{*}\mathcal{D}_{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{C}}
by left Kan extension.
Since
f^{*}
preserves coCartesian edges and
f^{*}_{T}\colon\mathcal{D}_{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{T}
admits a left adjoint, which we denote by
f_{T\sharp}
,
for each
T\in\mathcal{C}
since
T\rightarrow S
is smooth,
we have a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{op}}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{D}_{S}

of
(\widetilde{f^{*}})^{\mathrm{op}}
relative to
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
as in
B.11
.
The functor
f_{\sharp}
commutes with the left-tensor structure
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\times\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}

since
f_{\sharp}
is a certain twist of
f_{!}
.
Consider the composition

G\colon(X/S)^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{sm}}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{{}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{f_{\sharp}}\pi^{*}\mathcal{D}_{S}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}}\mathcal{D}_{S}^{\mathrm{op}}.

By
B.11
,
{}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}|_{(X/S)_{\mathrm{sm}}}
is equivalent to the composition

(X/S)^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{sm}}\xrightarrow{G\times\{R\}}\mathcal{D}_{S}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal{D}_{S}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Mor}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.
It will be sufficient to show that
G
is equivalent to
h_{!}\circ\mathrm{LKE}(R\circ\tau)
, where
h_{!}\colon\mathcal{D}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{S}
.
We will analyze
G
more closely.

By taking a suitbale model for
(-)^{\sim}
and taking
(-)^{\mathrm{op}}
,
we have a categorical fibration
(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{*}_{!})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{all}}

of Cartesian fibrations over
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
which preserves Cartesian edges.
Let
\mathrm{Ar}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{all}}\times_{\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}}\mathrm{Sm}_{S}
.
We consider the base change
r\colon(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{*}_{!})^{\mathrm{op}}\times_{\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{all}}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ar}^{\prime}

which is a map of Cartesian fibrations over
\mathrm{Sm}_{S}
.
This map is neither Cartesian nor coCartesian (and only categorical fibration by our choice).
However, we have:

(*)  Let
g\colon T\rightarrow T^{\prime}
be a
smooth
morphism in
\mathrm{Sm}_{S}
.
For any edge
e\colon\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ar}^{\prime}
over
g
and any object

\mathcal{F}\in(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{*}_{!})^{\mathrm{op}}\times_{\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{all}}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\prime}
such that
r(\mathcal{F})=e(0)
,
there exists an
r
-coCartesian edge
f\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
over
e
.

Indeed, by
[
A1
, 1.5]
, it suffices to show that the fiber
r_{g}:=r\times_{\mathrm{Sm}_{S},g}\Delta^{1}
is a coCartesian fibration.
To show this, we first show that
r_{g}
is a Cartesian fibration.
Indeed, we must check the conditions of
[
Lu
, 1.4.14]
.
The condition
[
Lu
, 1.4.14 (a)]
is easy.
For
Z\in\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
, the fiber
D_{Z}\colon(\mathcal{D}^{*}_{!})_{Z}\rightarrow\mathrm{All}^{\mathrm{op}}_{Z}\simeq\mathrm{Sch}_{/Z}

is a coCartesian fibration associated to the functor
\mathrm{Sch}_{/Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
sending
Y/Z
to
\mathcal{D}_{Y}
and
f
to
f_{!}
.
Since
f_{!}
admits a right adjoint,
f^{!}
,
D_{Z}
is in fact a Cartesian fibration as well by
[
HTT
, 5.2.2.5]
.
Since
(r_{g})_{0}=r_{T}
,
(r_{g})_{1}=r_{T^{\prime}}
are base changes of
D_{T}
,
D_{T^{\prime}}
, they are Cartesian fibrations.
Thus,
[
Lu
, 1.4.14 (b)]
is satisfied.
Since
f^{!}
for any morphism
f
and
g^{*}
commutes because
g
is assumed to be smooth, and
[
Lu
, 1.4.14 (c)]
follows.
Thus
r_{g}
is a Cartesian fibration as desired.
Note that up till now, we have not used that
g
is smooth.
To show that
r_{g}
is coCartesian, it suffices to show this over a fixed edge of
(\mathrm{Ar}^{\prime})_{g}
.
Assume we are given an edge of
(\mathrm{Ar}^{\prime})_{g}
depicted as

\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{U^{\prime}_{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{g}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{U^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{T^{\prime}.}

Unwinding the definition, the Cartesian pullback associated to this edge is
f^{!}\widetilde{g}^{*}
.
This admits a left adjoint
\widetilde{g}_{\sharp}f_{!}
, thus
r_{g}
is a coCartesian fibration as desired.

Let
(X/S)^{\unrhd}
be the (ordinary) category whose set of objects is
\mathrm{Obj}\bigl((X/S)_{\mathrm{sm}}\bigr)\sqcup\mathrm{Obj}(\mathrm{Sm}_{S})
.
The object corresponding to

is denoted by

.
The morphism is defined so that

is equal;

;

and

In other words, it is a Grothendieck construction of the functor

, where

The map

.
We have the functor

in

where

Consider the base change

\mathrm{LKE}_{k^{\prime}}(G^{\prime})
factors as

\textstyle{(X/S)_{\mathrm{sm}}\times\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{G^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{k^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{(X/S)_{\mathrm{sm}}\times\Delta^{\{0,1\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{\prime}|_{\Delta^{\{0,1\}}}}
\textstyle{(X/S)_{\mathrm{sm}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X/S)^{\unrhd}.}

Since
\beta
is presentable,
\mathrm{LKE}_{k^{\prime}}(G^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y}})
, the
\beta^{\prime}
-left Kan extension of
G^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y}}
along
k^{\prime}
,
coincides with
\mathrm{LKE}_{k^{\prime}}(G^{\prime})
.
Finally, in view of the diagram (

⋆

‣
C.5
) as well as invoking
[
HTT
, 4.3.1.15]
,
we have
\mathrm{LKE}_{k^{\prime}}(G^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\mathrm{LKE}(R\circ\tau)
.
Thus, we get the claim because
h^{!}R_{S}\simeq\omega_{X/S}
.
∎

C.6

Let us recall Brown-Gersten property (B.G.-property for short) of cdh-topology.
For the detail, see
[
V1
]
.
Let
S
be a noetherian separated scheme of finite dimension,
and let
P
be a collection of commutative diagrams
Q
in
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
, stable under isomorphisms, of the form:

(C.6.i)

\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\textstyle{X.}

Let
S_{P}
be the site whose underlying category is
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
, and whose topology is the
coarsest topology containing the sieves generated by the morphisms
\{A\rightarrow X,Y\rightarrow X\}
for some
Q\in P
.
On the other hand,
P
induces a collection of maps
G_{P}^{0}:=\{Y\sqcup_{B}A\rightarrow X\}_{Q\in P}
in
\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S})
.
Let
G_{P}
be the union of
G^{0}_{P}
and
\emptyset^{\prime}\rightarrow\emptyset
, where
\emptyset^{\prime}
is an initial object of

\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S})
and
\emptyset
is the presheaf represented by the initial object of
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
One of the favorable features of the theory is that we can detect sheaves on
S_{P}
easily, namely it possesses a B.G.-property:

Lemma
. —

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Assume we are given a collection of squares
P
which is regular complete and bounded

(
cf.
[
V1
]
for the terminologies
)
.
Let
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a functor such that the canonical morphism

\mathcal{F}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(Y)\times_{\mathcal{F}(B)}\mathcal{F}(A)
is an equivalence for any
Q
and
\mathcal{F}(\emptyset)
is an initial object.
Then
\mathcal{F}
is a
\mathcal{D}
-valued sheaf on
S_{P}
.

Proof.

By
[
HTT
, 5.1.5.6]
,
\mathcal{F}
extends to a colimit preserving functor

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\colon\mathcal{P}(S_{P})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
By assumption, this functor factors through
\overline{G}_{P}^{-1}\mathcal{P}(S_{P})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
By
[
V1
, 3.8]
,
\overline{G}_{P}^{-1}\mathcal{P}(S_{P})\simeq\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(S_{P})
.
By
[
HTT
, 5.5.4.20]
, we get that
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
factors through a colimit preserving functor

\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(S_{P})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{op}}
,
and the lemma follows.
∎

We consider the following types of squares.
A square
Q
is said to be a
blowup square
if the square is Cartesian,
e
is a closed immersion,
p
is proper,
and
p\times_{X}(X\setminus A)
is an isomorphism.
A square
Q
is said to be a
Nisnevich square
if the square is Cartesian,
e
is an open immersion,
p
is étale,
and
p\times_{X}(X\setminus A)_{\mathrm{red}}
is an isomorphism.
If we put
P_{\mathrm{cdh}}
to be the collection of blowup squares and Nisnevich squares, then
S_{P_{\mathrm{cdh}}}
is nothing but the cdh-site on
S
.
The main theorem of
[
V2
, 2.2]
states that
P_{\mathrm{cdh}}
is regular complete and bounded, and we may apply the previous lemma.

Remark
. —

If
P
is complete regular bounded, then
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(S_{P})
is compactly generated.
Indeed, since it is finitary (cf.
[
SAG
, A.3.1.1]
) by
[
V1
, just before 2.8]
,
the
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S,\mathrm{cdh}})
is coherent by
[
SAG
, A.3.1.3]
.
Moreover, it is of finite cohomological dimension by
[
V1
, 2.27]
, thus the claim follows.
In particular, if
S
is finite Krull dimensional,
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{/S,\mathrm{cdh}})
is compactly generated for any commutative ring
R
.

C.7 Lemma
. —

1.

Let
H\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{S}
be a functor such that;
1. For each object
f\colon T\rightarrow S
of
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
, we have
H(T)\simeq f_{*}f^{*}H(S)
;
2. For each morphism
T\rightarrow T^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
,
the morphism
H(T)\rightarrow H(T^{\prime})
is defined by the adjunction via the equivalence of the first condition.
Then
H
is a
\mathcal{D}_{S}
-valued cdh-sheaf.

2.

Let
G\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a functor over
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
which preserves coCartesian edges.
Then
M_{F}(R)\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is a cdh-sheaf.

3.

For each
Y\rightarrow T
in
(X/S)_{\mathrm{zar}}
, the functor

Y_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow(X/S)_{\mathrm{zar}}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{{}^{\mathrm{open}}\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}

is a
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-valued Zariski-sheaf on
Y
.

Proof.

Let us show the first claim.
By Lemma
C.6
, it suffices to check the following conditions:

1.

H(\emptyset)
is an initial object;

2.

For a blowup square (resp. Nisvevich square)
Q
of the form (
C.6.i
), the morphism

H(X)\rightarrow H(Y)\times_{H(B)}H(A)
is an equivalence;

The first condition is obvious, and the verification of the second condition is standard.
For the convenience of the reader, we recall an argument for the second condition.
We only treat the blowup square.
For an object
f\colon T\rightarrow S
of
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}
, let
\mathcal{F}_{T}:=f^{*}H(S)
.
Let
i=e\colon A\rightarrow X
be the closed immersion,

j\colon X\setminus A\rightarrow X
be the complement,
f=p\colon Y\rightarrow X
, and
h\colon B\rightarrow X
.
We must show that the map
\mathcal{F}_{X}\rightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{F}_{Y}\times_{h_{*}\mathcal{F}_{B}}i_{*}\mathcal{F}_{A}
is an equivalence.
Since the pair
(j_{!}j^{*},i_{*}i^{*})
is conservative (cf.
[
A1
, 6.1]
),
it suffices to check the equivalence after applying
j_{!}j^{*}
and
i_{*}i^{*}
separately.
This is easy by using localization sequence.

Let us show the second claim.
Let
\mathcal{H}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{S}
be the functor sending
f\colon T\rightarrow S
to
f_{*}R

defined by taking a
p
-right Kan extension of
R
.
By
B.10
and Corollary
Corollary
.
2
,
M_{G}(R)
is equivalent to the composition

\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\{G(S)\}\times\mathcal{H}^{*}}\mathcal{D}_{S}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal{D}_{S}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Mor}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.
Since
\mathcal{H}^{*}
is a
\mathcal{D}_{S}
-valued cdh-sheaf by the first claim, this composition is a
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-valued cdh-sheaf as required.
For the last claim, since Zariski topology also satisfy the B.G.-property with respect to Mayer-Vietoris squares,
and we may prove similarly.
∎

We finish this paper by providing proofs of the claims in
2.12
.

C.8
Proof of
2.12
.
1
and
2.12
.
2

The proofs of
2.12
.
1
and
2.12
.
2
are essentially the same,
so we only show
2.12
.
2
.
By taking a left Kan extension, we have the functor
Q_{f}^{*}
sending
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{T\times_{S}D}
over
T
to

f_{T}^{*}\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{T\times_{S}D^{\prime}}
, where
f_{T}\colon T\times_{S}D^{\prime}\rightarrow T\times_{S}D
is the projection.
We wish to show that this functor admits a left adjoint relative to
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}
(cf.
[
HA
, 7.3.2.2]
).
Recall that the map
\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/S}
is presentable.
We claim that the functor
Q_{f}^{*}
preserves Cartesian edges.
Let
\phi\colon T^{\prime}\rightarrow T
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
.
By
[
HTT
, 7.3.1.3]
, it suffices to show that the following diagram is right adjointable:

\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{T}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{T\times\Box}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\phi\times\mathrm{id})^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{T^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{T^{\prime}}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{T^{\prime}\times\Box}.}

In other words, we must check that the base change morphism
f_{T}^{*}\phi_{*}\rightarrow(\phi\times\mathrm{id})_{*}f_{T^{\prime}}^{*}
is an equivalence.
This is an equivalence since
f
is assumed to be smooth.
Now, by
[
HA
, 7.3.2.6]
and since
f^{*}_{T}
admits a left adjoint,
F
admits a relative left adjoint as desired.

Let us show the second claim.
First, observe that
a_{f!}
is left adjoint to
a_{f}^{*}
.
Thus, by taking the adjoint of the homotopy commutative diagram of
2.12
.
1
, we have the morphism of functors

Q_{f!}\circ q^{*}_{D^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\rightarrow q_{D}^{*}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\circ a_{f!}
.
It remains to show that this morphism is an equivalence, which can be checked pointwise.
This follows from the following properties: for a smooth morphism
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
of relative dimension
d
,
f_{!}\!\left<d\right>
is left adjoint to
f^{*}
;
for morphisms
X\xrightarrow{f}Y\xrightarrow{g}Z
, we have the canonical equivalence
g_{!}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(X/Y)\simeq\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(X/Z)
.
∎

C.9
Proof of
2.12
.
3

Once the morphism is defined, then the claim for the equivalence is classical since the verification is pointwise.
Let us construct the morphism.
By taking
(-)^{\mathrm{op}}
to the inclusion
\mathrm{Ar}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Sch}_{/S})
,
we have
\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}})
.
This is the functor sending
X\rightarrow T
to the edge
e\colon\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}

corresponding to the morphism
T\leftarrow X
in
\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}
(so
e(0)=T
and
e(1)=X
).
By taking the adjoint, we have the map

\mathrm{ar}\colon\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}})
.
Define a simplicial set
\mathcal{X}
by the following Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ar}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}}).}

By
[
HTT
, 3.1.2.1]
, the right vertical map is Cartesian as well as coCartesian.
Thus,
F
is also Cartesian and coCartesian.
Now, consider the functor
\mathrm{prod}\colon\mathrm{Ar}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ar}
sending
X\rightarrow T
to

\mathrm{pr}_{2}\colon X\times_{T}X\rightarrow X
.
One checks easily that this indeed defines a functor.
Let
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}):=\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\circ\mathrm{prod}\colon\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
,
which sends
X\rightarrow T
to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(X\times_{T}X/X)
.
This functor is a vertex of
\mathcal{X}_{1}
.
We define
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X})
to be the composition
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}\circ c
, where
c\colon\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}

is the functor sending
X\rightarrow T
to
X\rightarrow X
.
We have the functor
\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}})
sending
X\rightarrow T
to the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{prod}(X/T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\times_{T}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{c(X/T)}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{X.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}

Note here that
\Delta
is proper since
X\rightarrow T
is separated.
This defines a morphism of functors
\mathrm{prod}\rightarrow c
.
Thus, we have an edge
e_{1}\colon\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X})
in
\mathcal{X}_{1}
.
On the other hand, let
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{T}):=\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}
, which is a vertex of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
.

Now, let us construct an edge
e_{2}\colon\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{T})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X})
in
\mathcal{X}
.
We have the functor
D\colon\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}})
sending
X\rightarrow T

to the (Cartesian) diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{id}(X/T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{T}
\textstyle{\mathrm{prod}(X/T)}
\textstyle{X\times_{T}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\textstyle{X.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Consider the composition
E\colon\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{D}\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}}\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{D})
.
If we compose
E
with the canonical map
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Sch}_{/S}^{\mathrm{op}})
,
the composition coincides with
\mathrm{ar}
.
This implies that the adjoint
\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})
of
E
defines an edge of
\mathcal{X}
,
which we denote by
e_{2}
.

Let us finish the construction.
By definition, we have a Cartesian edge
\mathcal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{T})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X})
in
\mathcal{X}
.
What we have constructed can be summarized as the following diagram in
\mathcal{X}

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X})}
\textstyle{\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{1\},}

where
\rightsquigarrow
is a Cartesian edge.
Thus, we have an edge
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{T})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{*}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{T})
making the above diagram homotopy commutative.
∎
